# Deck review: every finding, deck by deck

This is the long list behind [DECK_REVIEW.md](../DECK_REVIEW.md). It was generated from the review run on the integration branch `claude/settings-topbar-icon-flvft1` at commit `1af29676`. Line numbers refer to that commit.

How each finding was checked:

- **confirmed**: a second agent re-read the code, re-ran the model or checked the source, and agreed. Where it corrected the proposed change, the corrected version is the one printed here.
- **uncertain**: the second agent could not settle it. Most of these need a look on the preview or a check against the guideline text.
- **render-checked**: rendered in Chromium at phone (390x844 @3) and desktop, sometimes iPad. The machine ran at 1 to 3 fps, so these checks show states, not smoothness.
- Refuted findings are left out (8 of 278).

Each entry gives the slide, the quote from the deck, the problem, the change and the source. A quote from a slide is the text as it is now.


## Foundations


### circulation (`src/ui/decks/circulation.js`)

A sound first deck. The anatomy is accurate, the HVPG and PPG definitions are right (Baveno VIII: CSPH at 10 mmHg or more), and the summary ends on an application question. The problems are wording and fit. Eight of the 11 lines run to two or three sentences, and one is a fragment. Five notes run past four sentences. The meal slide's notes say to watch a portal flow tile the slide does not have. Its line promises a near doubling of flow, but the model shows +29%. Four slides (lobule, triad, wall, central) teach lobule anatomy that the Lobule and Two inflows decks repeat. Merging two of them frees a slide for something no deck draws: where portal and systemic veins meet. Several asks test recall rather than use, and one repeats a quick-check question word for word.

**Keep:** The 'lenses' slide: one healthy patient colored four ways with lens buttons. It teaches the app's visual language once for every later deck.; The 'define' slide and the summary ask (HVPG 8 mmHg: portal hypertension, not yet clinically significant). Both test whether the learner can apply the fixed cut-offs.; The meal time-lapse with the minutes flow-time chart, the only use of that clock. Pressure is plotted on a scale from zero, so its small rise looks small.; The 'series' idea (two capillary beds in series), which sets up every later pre-, intra- and post-hepatic deck.

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `flow`
  - Now: Watch the portal flow tile and the vessels speed up as the meal is digested, while the portal pressure rises by under a millimeter.
  - Problem: The slide has tool flowtime and no data field, so there is no portal flow tile to watch. The notes also run to 5 sentences and 124 words, with a run-on ('...once the resistance has risen; in most portal hypertension the resistance rises first').
  - Change: flow notes: 'Watch the chart: portal flow climbs as the meal is digested, while portal pressure rises by under 1 mmHg, because healthy sinusoids barely resist. In cirrhosis the resistance is high, so the same meal raises the HVPG by a couple of mmHg; that is why inflow matters most once the resistance has risen, though in most portal hypertension the resistance rises first. Later the gut arterioles stay dilated, and the extra inflow keeps the pressure high even after collaterals open. Beta-blockers and octreotide cut the inflow, carvedilol also lowers the resistance, and a TIPS bypasses the liver.'
  - Source: src/ui/decks/circulation.js:73 (tool only, no data), :78; src/ui/presenter-tools.js:13-14. Postprandial HVPG rise in cirrhosis of about 26% (n=10, PMC3592829; I found it by search and did not read the full text).
- **Teaching, medium, confirmed**, slide `flow`
  - Now: Pressure equals flow times resistance. Normal sinusoids have so little resistance that nearly doubling the flow adds almost no pressure. Colored by flow, the gut veins and the portal vein deepen as the flow rises.
  - Problem: The line has three sentences. 'Pressure equals' should be 'the pressure drop equals' (the eq is ΔP = Q × R). It promises a near doubling of flow that the screen does not show: the model goes from 1.09 to 1.41 L/min, +29%. The caveat then apologizes ('about a quarter', also inexact). Live tokens would keep the line true to the screen.
  - Change: line: 'After a meal portal flow climbs to {pvFlow}, yet portal pressure rises only to {pv}, because healthy sinusoids barely resist; colored by flow, the gut veins and the portal vein deepen.' caveat (same text on circuit/meal, circuit.js:55): 'In people portal flow can nearly double after a meal; the model shows a rise of about 30%.'
  - Source: Model run of the deck chain (src/worker-core.js:331-357): pvFlow 1.09 to 1.41 L/min, pv 7.8 to 8.5 mmHg. Cox EF, Palaniyappan N, Aithal GP, Guha IN, Francis ST, Eur Radiol Exp 2018;2:26, doi 10.1186/s41747-018-0056-3 (MRI: portal flow +137% in 10 healthy volunteers, +67% in compensated cirrhosis).
- **Humanizer, medium, confirmed**, slide `ladder`
  - Now: About {pv} in the [portal vein](pv) and {ra} in the [right atrium](ra). Healthy [sinusoids](sin) barely resist, so the portal pressure gradient is only {ppg}. Colored by pressure drop, every vein stays pale.
  - Problem: The first sentence is a fragment with no verb. The line runs to three sentences, and it never gives the abbreviation PPG, which the ladder's bracket then uses.
  - Change: line: 'Healthy [sinusoids](sin) barely resist, so the portal pressure gradient (PPG), from the [portal vein](pv) to the [IVC](ivc), is only {ppg}; colored by pressure drop, every vein stays pale.' (29 words; the ladder shows the station values)
  - Source: docs/ai-humanizer.md:81-83 (one idea per line, define a term on first use), :115 (full sentences); deck-authoring-guide.md:489 rule 1
- **Humanizer, medium, confirmed**, slide `series`
  - Now: The first bed is the capillaries of the gut and spleen. The second is the liver's sinusoids. Because they are in series, resistance in the liver or between it and the heart raises pressure in every vein upstream.
  - Problem: Three short sentences in the same rhythm make one idea.
  - Change: line: 'The first bed is the capillaries of the gut and spleen and the second the liver’s sinusoids, so resistance in the liver or between it and the heart raises the pressure in every vein upstream.' (35 words; keeps the sinusoids and heart terms)
  - Source: docs/ai-humanizer.md:75 (same-rhythm sentences), deck-authoring-guide.md:489 rule 1
- **Humanizer, medium, confirmed**, slide `define`
  - Now: In cirrhosis it is measured as the HVPG, and in this case it is {hvpg}. An HVPG of 6 to 9 mmHg is subclinical. From 10 mmHg, varices and ascites become possible, and the risk rises with the gradient.
  - Problem: Three sentences, an unclear 'it' twice, and a typed cut-off where the house style uses an outlined rule pill.
  - Change: line: 'In cirrhosis the gradient is measured as the HVPG, {hvpg} in this case; clinically significant portal hypertension starts at {>=10 mmHg}, where varices and ascites become possible and the risk climbs with the gradient.' (34 words; keep 6 to 9 mmHg subclinical in the notes)
  - Source: deck-authoring-guide.md:212 (cut-off pills), :494 rule 6; tests/deck-thresholds.test.js:24-35
- **Overlap, medium, uncertain**, slide `lobule`
  - Now: The classic lobule is a hexagon about a millimeter across, with a portal tract at its corners and a central vein (terminal hepatic venule) in the middle.
  - Problem: Four of the 11 slides (lobule, triad, wall, central) are lobule anatomy. The lobule notes repeat lobule/lobule almost word for word. The triad slide repeats two-inflows/meet ('The portal tract holds a portal venule, a hepatic arteriole and a bile ductule').
  - Change: If merged: terms { 'portal venule': 'lobule:triad', 'hepatic arteriole': 'lobule:arteriole', 'bile ductule': 'lobule:ductule', 'central vein': 'lobule:central' }, keep pill: false, and move the one-cell plates and the bile-flow facts into the notes. Otherwise keep both slides and only trim the shared notes sentence.
  - Why uncertain: The overlap is real but overstated. circulation/lobule and lobule/lobule share only their first sentence (circulation.js:48, lobule.js:23). Circulation is a foundation deck that has to stand alone. The merge loses two facts: 'Liver cells line the sinusoids in plates one cell thick' and 'Bile leaves through the ductule, against the direction of blood flow'. The merged line uses singular 'portal venule' and 'hepatic arteriole', but the slide's terms keys are plural, so the pills would not match. A bile ductule may also be too small to see at cam 'lobule:fit'.
  - Source: src/ui/decks/circulation.js:45-57; lobule.js:20-24; two-inflows.js:30-34
- **Accuracy, low, confirmed**, slide `summary`
  - Now: Yes, it is above 5 mmHg. It is not clinically significant until 10 mmHg, so decompensation is less likely, though not ruled out.
  - Problem: The answer is correct. It misses the main current exception, which Sites already teaches.
  - Change: Add: 'In MASLD the HVPG can read below the true portal pressure.'
  - Source: Ferrusquia-Acosta J et al., J Hepatol 2021;74:811-818 (PMID 33068638); src/ui/decks/sites.js:57
- **Humanizer, low, confirmed**, slide `inflow`
  - Now: Colored by origin, each vein keeps its source’s color.
  - Problem: The sentence tells the learner nothing they can use; it could sit on any slide colored by origin. It also makes the line two sentences, and the notes end by repeating it (5 sentences).
  - Change: line: 'The splenic and superior mesenteric veins join behind the pancreas to form the portal vein, which brings the liver about three quarters of its blood; colored by origin, splenic and gut blood run side by side.' Cut the last notes sentence.

- **Humanizer, low, confirmed**, slide `wall`
  - Now: Fenestrae about 100 nm wide and no basement membrane let plasma and protein into the space of Disse. With protein on both sides, oncotic pressure does little to hold fluid back, so higher sinusoidal pressure means more lymph.
  - Problem: The line has two sentences. The notes have 6 sentences and 116 words (the rule allows 2 to 4). The 100 nm figure is correct.
  - Change: As proposed, but notes sentence 2: 'Because protein crosses the wall, oncotic pressure barely opposes filtration, so a rise in sinusoidal pressure turns straight into protein-rich lymph, where most ascites in portal hypertension begins.'
  - Source: Wisse E et al., Gene Ther 2008 (human sinusoidal fenestrae, mean 107 nm); deck-authoring-guide.md:499 rule 11
- **Teaching, low, confirmed**, slide `inflow`
  - Now: Which veins form the portal vein?
  - Problem: This ask duplicates the first quick check ('Which two veins join to form the portal vein?'), so the deck tests the same recall twice. The 'central' ask ('Where does blood go after the central vein?') is also pure recall.
  - Change: inflow ask: ['A drug is swallowed. Why does the liver meet it before the rest of the body does?', 'Gut blood drains through the portal vein to the liver first, so absorbed drugs, toxins and bacterial products pass the liver before they reach the systemic circulation.']. central ask: ['The right atrial pressure is 18 mmHg. What happens to the sinusoidal pressure, and why?', 'It rises too: nothing between the sinusoids and the atrium holds the pressure back, so it passes straight back through the hepatic and central veins.']
  - Source: src/ui/decks/circulation.js:10, :20, :70

### circuit (`src/ui/decks/circuit.js`)

The strongest foundation deck. It applies one law (ΔP = Q × R) on the circuit view throughout, with resistor readouts, a live scarring slider, a propranolol/carvedilol switch and a summary that reads every change as Q or R. The model supports its claims. Carvedilol lowers the HVPG 1.3 mmHg more than propranolol in decompensated cirrhosis; meta-analyses give 0.8 to 2.2 mmHg. A TIPS takes the PPG from 17.8 to 9.7 mmHg. Fixes needed: the meal and 'both' slides show different tiles (PPG, then HVPG), so the 'about twice' claim cannot be checked on screen, and the notes contradict the model on flow. 'Halves' should be a live value. The biggest gap is forward flow. The blueprint's first learning goal, the hyperdynamic circulation, has no slide anywhere in the app, although the engine computes it.

Render check: Item 7 is confirmed and harmless on screen. The 8-day cirrhotic meal grows ascites from 3 to 844 mL. The varix widens from 3.9 to 5.6 mm, but that part is an acute distension that also appears without days. Neither shows anywhere on the slide: the tiles carry flow and HVPG only, the circuit draws pressures (Varices node 16.7 then 16.6 mmHg), and the summary row uses flow, resistance and PPG. The only trace of remodeling a learner could notice is the collateral resistance changing from ×13 to ×12.

**Keep:** Every slide on view 'circuit' with rLiver, rColl and rGut readouts: the only deck where resistance is a number the learner can watch.; The 'resist' scarring slider: an acute change in resistance is exactly what a slider can show honestly.; The propranolol/carvedilol compare, with delta against the untreated patient. The model's gap (HVPG 15.1 vs 13.8 mmHg) sits within the published range.; The summary table with a vs comparison on each row and the 'Advanced cirrhosis, no collaterals' extra row, which proves the parallel-resistor point in numbers (PPG 26.2 vs 17.8 mmHg).; Starting the 'both' lapse from 'resist' is correct: same patient, 11.6 rising to 13.3 mmHg; The circuit's resistance multipliers (Liver resistance ×3.7)

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `both`
  - Now: The rise is about twice as large, because the extra flow now crosses a higher resistance.
  - Problem: 'meal' shows pvFlow and PPG; 'both' shows pvFlow and HVPG. The learner therefore compares a PPG rise (+0.8 mmHg) with an HVPG rise (+1.7 mmHg). In the model the PPG rise is 2.6 times the healthy one (+0.8 vs +2.1 mmHg) and the HVPG rise 3.4 times. The notes say 'in cirrhosis the meal raises flow less', but the tiles show the same rise in flow, +0.32 L/min in both livers.
  - Change: both: tiles: ['pvFlow', 'ppg'] (as on 'meal'). line: 'The same rise in flow now crosses a higher resistance, so the gradient climbs more than twice as much.' notes, sentence 3: 'In patients the meal raises flow less in cirrhosis and part of it leaves through collaterals, so the rise is smaller than the resistance alone predicts.' caveat: 'In the model the meal raises flow by the same amount in both livers; in cirrhosis it rises less.'
  - Source: Model run: meal ppg 4.3 to 5.1, pvFlow 1.09 to 1.41; both ppg 13.6 to 15.7, hvpg 11.6 to 13.3, pvFlow 1.16 to 1.48. Cox EF et al., Eur Radiol Exp 2018;2:26 (portal flow +137% in healthy volunteers vs +67% in compensated cirrhosis).
- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `tips`
  - Now: Colored by flow, it carries most of the portal blood, and the gradient halves.
  - Problem: 'Halves' is a typed number (the model goes from 17.8 to 9.7 mmHg). The notes' 'The gradient falls below 12 mmHg' states a target as if it were automatic. EASL 2025 asks for the smallest diameter that reaches it. The title is identical to treatment/tips.
  - Change: line: 'A covered stent beside the sinusoids resists far less than they or any collateral, so, colored by flow, it carries most of the portal blood and the portal pressure gradient falls to {ppg}.' (33 words). Title and notes as proposed.
  - Source: EASL Clinical Practice Guidelines on TIPS, J Hepatol 2025;83:177-210 (doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.01.029); Wang Q et al., J Hepatol 2017;67:508-516; model run: RA 4.65 to 5.87 mmHg with TIPS
- **Out of date, medium, uncertain**, slide `drugs`
  - Now: In patients carvedilol's extra effect comes with a lower blood pressure, which matters once ascites appears.
  - Problem: This reads as a reason to avoid carvedilol once ascites appears. Baveno VIII now prefers carvedilol in patients with ascites and varices and gives explicit stop rules. The same notes state the stellate-cell mechanism as fact, while the Beta-blockers deck hedges it.
  - Change: notes: '…Carvedilol adds α1 blockade, which is thought to relax the stellate cells and lower the dynamic part of R. Switch between them: carvedilol lowers HVPG more with less fall in portal flow. It also lowers the blood pressure a little, so with ascites the dose is lowered or the drug stopped if systolic pressure stays under 90 mmHg, MAP under 65 mmHg, or HRS-AKI develops.' Drop 'The tiles compare with the untreated patient.' (stays at 5; merge the first two to reach 4)
  - Why uncertain: The current sentence ('comes with a lower blood pressure, which matters once ascites appears', circuit.js:94) is not wrong, and the stop rules support it. The new Baveno VIII statement preferring carvedilol with ascites and varices is medium confidence in digest A and low in digest B, and the rewrite drops 'and varices'. It also makes the notes 6 sentences. The mechanism hedge is valid: beta-blockers.js:47 says 'is thought to relax'.
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (new statement, LoE 3, strong, 81%; wording from journal-page extracts, the full text was not read); src/ui/decks/beta-blockers.js:32
- **Addition, medium, confirmed**, slide `new`
  - Now: In cirrhosis the splanchnic arterioles dilate for good (nitric oxide, glucagon, bacterial products), so the inflow stays high: the hyperdynamic circulation.
  - Problem: The blueprint's first goal (forward flow and the hyperdynamic circulation) and its curriculum lesson 4 have no slide in any deck. 'Hyperdynamic' appears once, in these notes, and the co tile is used only in Pregnancy. The engine computes it. From healthy to decompensated cirrhosis: - gut arteriole resistance falls from 74 to 44 mmHg·min/L; - splanchnic inflow rises from 1.10 to 1.56 L/min; - cardiac output rises from 5.0 to 6.7 L/min and heart rate from 60 to 89/min; - MAP holds at 93 to 94 mmHg.
  - Change: As proposed, with line: 'As cirrhosis advances the gut arterioles dilate, so more blood enters the gut and the heart pumps {co} at a normal blood pressure, a state called the hyperdynamic circulation.' caveat: 'The model has no kidneys; it adds the retained plasma volume directly.' notes, sentence 2: 'More blood enters the portal system, and most of it leaves through the collaterals.' Write 'in the model their resistance falls by about 40%'. ask: ['At rest the heart rate is 89/min. Why?', 'The dilated splanchnic and systemic arterioles lower the resistance the heart pumps against, so it pumps more and faster to hold the blood pressure.']
  - Source: docs/DESIGN_BLUEPRINT.md:99-100, :960; model run of preset cirr-decomp (src/engine/scenario.js:81); co token valid, src/ui/table-marks.js:65; Schrier RW et al., Hepatology 1988;8:1151-1157 (peripheral arterial vasodilation hypothesis)
- **Engine opportunity, medium, confirmed**, slide `where`

### two-inflows (`src/ui/decks/two-inflows.js`)

A focused, well-ordered deck that makes the hepatic arterial buffer response visible through the hepatic artery Doppler. The model backs its numbers: arterial flow doubles after a portal vein clot (0.40 to 0.86 L/min), and with the buffer off liver flow falls to 38%. The main faults are claims about things the figure cannot show. The hepatic artery is modeled but never drawn, yet two lines describe it on the plate. 'Total liver blood flow' misnames a tile that measures flow through the sinusoids. Two of the four case cards nearly copy cases in Sites, Shunts and Doppler. Most lines run to three sentences. A clot slider and a hepatic artery flow tile would turn the deck's central quantity into a number.

Render check: Item 2 is partly confirmed. After 'Buffer off' the liver-flow tile stops updating 7.5 to 9 s after the press, as the 7000 ms liveSet loop predicts. The frozen value was 39%, 31% and 33% on desktop and 31% on phone, against a settled model value of 38.5%. The claimed 55 to 60% never appeared. The number depends on where the breath and the ease are when the loop ends. On phone the tile is hidden anyway, and only the hepatic artery Doppler shows.

**Keep:** The hepatic artery Doppler with delta ghosts (HA('dual'), HA('cirr')) standing in for the undrawn artery: the learner sees the buffer as a faster waveform.; The 'switch' compare (buffer on, buffer off): a counterfactual only a model can show. The 'about 40%' matches the model (38.5%).; The 'artery' transplant case, which makes 'nothing buffers a fall in arterial flow' clinical through biliary ischemia.; The closing ask (hepatic artery velocity doubled after a TIPS: expected, not a complication), which transfers the idea to Doppler practice.; The hepatic artery Doppler beside the tile: 128 cm/s, RI 0.44-0.48 with the buffer on, against 54-57 cm/s, RI 0.58-0.66 with it off, shows the mechanism directly

- **Accuracy, high, confirmed**, slide `switch`
  - Now: Colored by flow, the hepatic artery and sinusoids thin.
  - Problem: A_HEP and its branches are in HIDDEN_EDGES, so there is no artery on the plate to thin. The notes repeat the claim ('On the figure, colored by flow, the hepatic artery and the sinusoids thin'). The 'portal' camera frames the confluence and splenic vein and may leave the sinusoids out. The line also runs to three sentences.
  - Change: switch line: 'With the buffer switched off, which no patient can do, the artery cannot open, so flow through the liver falls to about 40% of normal instead of {liver}.' (28 words, pill: false; {liver} is the slide's own 72%). Last notes sentence as proposed.
  - Source: src/ui/anatomy.js:15-20 (A_HEP, A_HR, A_HL, AP_R, AP_L hidden); src/ui/decks.js:103 (portal region); model run: liver 71.8% with the buffer, 38.5% with habrStrength 0
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `dual`
  - Now: Each brings about half of the oxygen. Colored by origin, the two inflows enter the liver separately.
  - Problem: The artery is not drawn, so the learner sees only the portal vein entering the liver. The arterial color appears only mixed into the sinusoids. The notes' 'The Doppler on the right' is wrong on a phone, where the data card sits below the figure.
  - Change: line: 'About three quarters of the liver’s blood arrives by the [portal vein](pv), {pvFlow} here, and a quarter by the hepatic artery, yet each brings about half of the liver’s oxygen.' Keep lens 'origin' only if the preview shows the arterial share in the sinusoids; otherwise drop it here, since 'meet' uses it. notes: 'The Doppler in the data card is the hepatic artery...'
  - Source: src/ui/anatomy.js:15-20; model run: hepatic artery 0.40 L/min against portal 1.09 L/min (27%)

  - Problem: The 'liver' tile is flow through the sinusoids as a share of normal. After a TIPS the artery carries 0.88 L/min, yet the tile reads 31%, because arterial blood leaves through the reversed portal branches to the stent. 'Total liver blood flow' misnames it. 'clot', 'artery' and 'cirr' also say 'liver blood flow'. The line has three sentences.
  - Change: tips line: 'After a TIPS the intrahepatic portal branches turn toward the stent and the buffer opens the artery, yet flow through the liver tissue falls to {liver} of normal; colored by origin, the sinusoids carry almost only arterial blood.' ### hvpg (`src/ui/decks/hvpg.js`)

The technique section is the deck's strength: the catheter goes route, free, wedge, result, with the live monitor. The clinical facts are mostly right. The clear error is the 'grades' ask, which says varices bleed at '12 mmHg or more'. That treats 12 as a cut-off, which the same slide's notes, the authoring guide and the humanizer all reject. The objectives repeat the error. The 'hvpg' notes quote the 25 kPa rule-in without its etiology limits and leave out the MASLD caveat that the wedge can read low. The sites deck already carries that caveat. The case gives no reason why this patient needs a catheter today. The 'Where HVPG misleads' section repeats sites and where-resistance almost slide for slide, with the same summary columns. The deck could swap some of that repetition for things only a measuring deck can show: a periportal-fibrosis slider and a live beta-blocker response test.

**Keep:** The catheter sequence (cath route, free, wedge, result, blocked, with monitor) is the only place the engine shows how an HVPG is actually measured; keep the order and the monitor carrying over between slides.; The applied asks with numbers: 'WHVP 21, FHVP 20, right atrium 19 mmHg' on 'heart' and 'falls from 18 to 13 mmHg' on 'hvpg'. They test reasoning, not recall.; The 'grades' scale visual with four patients placed by HVPG; the notes carry the correct 'not a cut-off' wording for 12 mmHg.; The 'heart' notes line 'The same pattern with a normal right atrial pressure points to resistance between the hepatic veins and the heart, such as an IVC web' is a good bedside rule.

- **Accuracy, high, confirmed**, slide `grades`
  - Now: ask: ['At what HVPG can varices bleed?', '12 mmHg or more.']
  - Problem: This teaches 12 mmHg as a cut-off below which varices cannot bleed. The same slide's notes say '12 is not a cut-off, and varices can bleed below it'. Guide 8.6 says 'rising risk, not a cut-off: varices can bleed below it', and the humanizer says to word thresholds as rising risk.
  - Change: ask: ['Small varices are found at an HVPG of 11 mmHg. Can they bleed?', 'Yes, though rarely: the risk rises steeply from about 12 mmHg, and size, red signs and liver function count as much as the number.']
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (HVPG statements carried forward from Baveno VII, de Franchis R et al., J Hepatol 2022;76:959-974); docs/deck-authoring-guide.md 8.6 'Key facts for text'; docs/ai-humanizer.md 'Keep thresholds as rising risk'
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `deck`
  - Now: objectives: [..., 'Calculate the HVPG and state its 5, 10 and 12 mmHg cut-offs', ...]
  - Problem: This objective calls 12 mmHg a cut-off, the same error as hvpg-1, so the Outline slide teaches it before the talk begins.
  - Change: 'Calculate the HVPG, give its 5 and 10 mmHg cut-offs, and know that bleeding risk climbs from about 12'
  - Source: Same as hvpg-1
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `hvpg`
  - Now: notes: '... In this case the HVPG is about 12 mmHg, which is clinically significant. ... After a variceal bleed, a fall on treatment to below 12 mmHg, or by 20% or more, protects against rebleeding. ... Without a catheter, liver stiffness (25 kPa or more) rules clinically significant portal hypertension in; stiffness of 15 kPa or less with platelets of 150 or more rules it out.'
  - Problem: (1) The 25 kPa rule-in is given without its limits. Baveno VIII applies it only to viral, alcohol-related and non-obese MASLD cACLD; obese MASLD uses ANTICIPATE-NASH at 75% or more, or a spleen stiffness over 55 kPa at 100 Hz. (2) The notes leave out that the wedge can underestimate portal pressure in MASLD. sites.js:57 says so, but the deck about HVPG does not. (3) 'about 12 mmHg' is typed, while the model shows 11.6. (4) The rebleeding target is classically 12 mmHg or less, not 'below 12'. (5) At 6 sentences, the notes exceed rule 11's 2-4.
  - Change: Use the reviewer's notes, but end: 'Without a catheter, liver stiffness and the platelet count stand in for it.'
  - Source: Ferrusquia-Acosta J et al., J Hepatol 2021;74:811-818 (PMID 33068638); Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (rule-in criteria); Villanueva C et al., PREDESCI, Lancet 2019;393:1597-1608; de Franchis R et al., Baveno VII 2022; model HVPG 11.6 computed with createCore 'sequence' (src/worker-core.js), as tests/deck-states.js does
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `ppg`
  - Now: line: 'This portal vein clot lies before the sinusoids, so only a direct portal reading shows it. The PPG is {ppg}. Colored by pressure drop, the large drop sits at the clot, not in the sinusoids.'
  - Problem: 'Only a direct portal reading shows it' is wrong in practice. Doppler or CT shows the clot. A direct portal reading is the only way to measure the gradient, and it is rarely needed for a clot. The line also has three sentences.
  - Change: line: 'This portal vein clot lies before the sinusoids, so the wedge misses it and only a direct portal reading measures the gradient: the PPG is {ppg}. Colored by pressure drop, the drop sits at the clot.' Notes addition as proposed.
  - Source: EASL CPG on vascular diseases of the liver, J Hepatol 2026;84:399-456; Northup PG et al., AASLD vascular guidance, Hepatology 2021;73:366-413
- **Teaching, medium, confirmed**, slide `route`
  - Now: vignette: 'A 57-year-old man with alcohol-associated cirrhosis has platelets of 88,000/µL and a liver stiffness of 24 kPa. He has never had ascites or bleeding, and he is referred for HVPG measurement.'
  - Problem: The case gives no reason for a catheter. Today this man would be treated as having CSPH without one: AASLD 2024 presumes CSPH at 20-25 kPa with platelets under 150, and an ANTICIPATE probability at 24 kPa with platelets of 88 is very likely 75% or more. He would start carvedilol. A sharp resident will ask 'why measure?', and the deck never says when HVPG is still used.
  - Change: vignette: 'A 57-year-old man with alcohol-associated cirrhosis has platelets of 88,000/µL and a liver stiffness of 24 kPa. He has never had ascites or bleeding. Before resection of a 2 cm HCC, his surgeon asks for an HVPG.' (211 characters; the model state is unchanged). Add to the route notes: 'Today the HVPG is measured mostly before liver resection, when the bedside tests disagree, and in trials.' An alternative reason is a transjugular biopsy for an unclear diagnosis.
  - Source: Kaplan DE et al., AASLD Practice Guidance, Hepatology 2024;79:1180-1211; CSPH as a selection criterion for HCC resection: EASL HCC CPG and BCLC 2022 (not re-verified here); docs/deck-authoring-guide.md section 14 (case under 220 characters, must agree with model: plt 88.2, LSM 24.1 computed)

## Causes


### sites (`src/ui/decks/sites.js`)

A solid core deck: each of the six levels gets one patient and a pressure-drop picture, and the deck closes with ladders and a table. The vignettes match the model's labs. Its weak points are definitional and cross-deck. CSPH is tied to the PPG instead of the HVPG. The Budd–Chiari HVPG is shown as a measured 0.3 mmHg, while the hvpg and outflow decks call it unmeasurable. The ladders line leaves out the postsinusoidal level, and the SOS cut-off is worded 'above 10'. Five lines run to three sentences and two notes run past four. Engine wins are cheap: works/misleads brackets on every level slide, a central-vein slider on the SOS slide, and a blanked BCS row in the table. Every number quoted below comes from a run of the deck sequence through src/worker-core.js.

**Keep:** One patient per level, each with lens 'drop' and a rail, and what-if sliders on pvt (clot size) and presin (periportal fibrosis): the room can drag the resistance and watch the HVPG ignore it.; The cardiac slide's 'Normal heart' patient button is the app's only compare-by-patient, and it works well here.; presin ask (open portal vein, HVPG 3 mmHg, normal free and atrial pressures) and the summary ask (SAAG 1.6, protein 4 g/dL) test reasoning, not recall.; The vignettes quote labs the model reproduces (platelets 57,000/µL in cirrhosis; SAAG 1.4 and protein 4.3 g/dL in Budd–Chiari and heart failure).

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `overview`
  - Now: Portal hypertension is a portal pressure gradient above 5 mmHg; 10 mmHg or more is clinically significant.
  - Problem: CSPH is defined by the HVPG (10 mmHg or more), not by the PPG. The app's fixed PPG cut-offs are 6 (amber) and 12 (red), so 'a PPG of 10 or more is clinically significant' conflicts with both the guideline and the house definitions.
  - Change: notes: 'Portal hypertension is a portal pressure gradient above 5 mmHg; clinically significant portal hypertension is an HVPG of 10 mmHg or more.'
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (HVPG statements carried forward from Baveno VII, J Hepatol 2022;76:959-974); docs/deck-authoring-guide.md section 9 rule 4
- **Overlap, medium, confirmed**, slide `summary`
  - Now: of: [{ preset: 'healthy', kicker: 'Reference', title: 'Healthy', ref: true }, ...SIX]
  - Problem: The table prints WHVP 25.5, FHVP 25.2 and HVPG 0.3 mmHg for Budd–Chiari as if measured. hvpg/summary blanks those cells ('Cannot be measured'), and outflow/summary calls the HVPG 'meaningless'. Learners see three answers to one question.
  - Change: Write the summary's of explicitly: of: [{ preset: 'healthy', kicker: 'Reference', title: 'Healthy', ref: true }, 'pvt', 'presin', 'sin', 'postsin', { id: 'post', blank: ['whvp', 'fhvp', 'hvpg'] }, 'cardiac']. Leave SIX for the ladders slide. Add to the summary notes: 'With clotted hepatic veins the catheter usually cannot wedge, so the HVPG is left blank; the PPG and imaging make the diagnosis.' On 'post', keep the hedge in the line (see sites-6).
  - Source: src/ui/decks/hvpg.js:88 (blank: ['whvp','fhvp','hvpg']); src/ui/decks/outflow.js:85-86; model run: budd-chiari WHVP 25.5, FHVP 25.2, HVPG 0.3, PPG 22.3
- **Engine opportunity, medium, uncertain**, slide `pvt, presin, sin, postsin, post, cardiac`
  - Now: data: 'ladder', key: ['hvpg', 'ppg'], rail: true
  - Problem: Objective 2 is 'Predict the HVPG for resistance at each level', yet no level slide uses the ladder's works/misleads brackets. The where deck uses them on every answer slide.
  - Change: Add brackets on pvt { hvpg: 'misleads', ppg: 'works' }, presin { hvpg: 'misleads', ppg: 'works' }, sin { hvpg: 'works', ppg: 'works' }, postsin { hvpg: 'works', ppg: 'works' } and post { hvpg: 'misleads', ppg: 'works' }. Leave cardiac without brackets unless the owner wants heart slides app-wide to show 'misleads'.
  - Why uncertain: presenter.js:801 supports brackets, and they fit pvt, presin, post (misleads/works) and sin, postsin (works/works). Model: SOS HVPG 8.9, PPG 10.3. The cardiac pair 'misleads/misleads' goes against a pattern that looks deliberate across the app: no heart slide has brackets (hvpg.js:72 heart, right-heart.js, where.js:62 a3), and hvpg/summary notes heart failure as 'Normal, all pressures high', not as misleading. In heart failure a normal PPG correctly predicts no varices.
  - Source: src/ui/presenter.js:801 (ladder.set takes brackets); src/ui/decks/where.js:32,47,92,107
- **Engine opportunity, medium, confirmed**, slide `postsin`
  - Now: An HVPG above 10 mmHg, measured with a transjugular biopsy, strongly supports the diagnosis. The model shows an earlier stage, with an HVPG of about 9 mmHg: raised, but under 10.
  - Problem: The notes apologize for the model sitting under the diagnostic cut-off, and the threshold wording ('above 10') differs from EBMT 2023 ('10 mmHg or more'). A slider lets the room take the same patient past 10. In the model, central-vein resistance of 20 gives HVPG 8.9, 32 gives 11.3 and 40 gives 12.6.

### prehepatic (`src/ui/decks/prehepatic.js`)

Short and accurate, with good notes on the work-up (JAK2, SMV extension, red flags for bowel ischemia) and an honest note that the catheter is rarely needed. It is the only deck in this group without a live control, and its first line does not say what its title promises: no Doppler finding and no live value. The main gap is treatment. The guideline cornerstone, immediate anticoagulation for at least 6 months, is only in the notes, although the engine can play it as a time-lapse, and running the clot patient for 240 days reproduces the cavernoma preset exactly. Splenic vein thrombosis can show the unused 'change' lens and a splenectomy/embolization compare. Adding one slide gives 7 slides in about 9 minutes.

**Keep:** clot notes: look for MPN (JAK2), thrombophilia and local inflammation; red flags for bowel ischemia; anticoagulation at once for at least 6 months (Baveno VII, EASL 2025, AASLD 2020).; cath notes: 'In practice the diagnosis is made on imaging, and the catheter is rarely needed.' It tells the truth about the ladder.; The svt title ('A splenic vein clot raises the pressure on the left only') and its cause-and-cure ask.; The summary line: the HVPG stays normal throughout; the PPG rises only when the portal vein carries the resistance.

- **Addition, high, confirmed**, slide `new`
  - Now: (new slide between 'cath' and 'cavernoma')
  - Problem: Anticoagulation is the main treatment for acute non-cirrhotic PVT, but no slide shows it. The engine's anticoag param lyses the clot during a lapse (from the clot slide: PV 28.8 to 7.8 mmHg by day 180; partly open at day 90 with a fast 47 cm/s jet).
  - Change: notes: '…for at least 6 months, and long-term when a prothrombotic condition persists or the bowel was threatened. In a prospective European cohort, by the end of follow-up the portal vein had reopened in 39% of treated patients, the splenic vein in 80% and the superior mesenteric vein in 73%…' Keep the caveat 'about 4 in 10'.
  - Source: Plessier A et al., Hepatology 2010;51:210-218 (median 234 days: PV 39%, SV 80%, SMV 73% patent; ascites HR 3.8, splenic vein occlusion HR 3.5): https://pure.eur.nl/en/publications/acute-portal-vein-thrombosis-unrelated-to-cirrhosis-a-prospective/; EASL CPG vascular diseases of the liver 2025 (J Hepatol 2026;84:399-456); Baveno VIII vascular panel (LMWH then DOAC, per search excerpt, medium confidence); src/engine/engine.js:810-815; model runs

### ncph (`src/ui/decks/ncph.js`)

A compact, clear deck. Its biopsy-before-the-catheter-comes-out ask and the statement that the stiffness-and-platelet rules do not apply in PSVD are excellent. Its main problem is the one guideline claim it makes, on the nsbb slide: I could not verify that EASL 2025 or Baveno VIII say anything about carvedilol in presinusoidal disease. The model can show the point live instead: carvedilol lowers the portal pressure only 0.3 mmHg more than propranolol here, against 1.3 mmHg in CSPH. On the tract slide, the line says the sinusoids are spared while the tile beside it reads 'High' (9.5 mmHg). PSVD, the commonest Western cause, has no case of its own. The deck also lacks how schistosomiasis is diagnosed, and it lacks the mixed schistosomiasis plus HCV picture. Four additions take it to 10 slides.

**Keep:** wedge ask: transjugular biopsy before the catheter comes out. It tests application, not recall.; stiff notes: 'The stiffness and platelet rules that spare a scope do not apply in porto-sinusoidal vascular disorder: screen with endoscopy at diagnosis' (correct per Baveno VII and EASL 2025).; psvd notes: the right VALDIG histology (obliterative portal venopathy, NRH, incomplete septal fibrosis) and associations, and PVT as a common complication.; The summary table sets presinusoidal disease against CSPH, with an after-treatment row.

- **Accuracy, high, uncertain**, slide `nsbb`
  - Now: Propranolol cuts gut inflow: portal vein pressure falls to {pv}. EASL 2025 and Baveno VIII see no proven advantage for carvedilol when resistance is presinusoidal.
  - Problem: I found no EASL 2025 or Baveno VIII statement on carvedilol in presinusoidal or PSVD portal hypertension. Both literature digests also failed to find one. The only trial evidence is a 61-patient RCT in schistosomiasis (carvedilol against propranolol, rebleeding 3.3% against 10%, not significant). The caveat ('its extra effect is sinusoidal') is ambiguous on a propranolol slide.
  - Change: Keep the caveat. Line: 'Propranolol cuts gut inflow, so the portal vein pressure falls to {pv}; carvedilol has no proven advantage when the resistance is presinusoidal.' Restore 'EASL 2025 and Baveno VIII' only after the owner checks the PDFs. Notes may add the Madagascar trial (Razafindrazoto, JGH Open 2022, n = 61), which the reviewer verified and I could not re-check.
  - Why uncertain: The head commit 1af29676 changed this line on purpose: 'Both guidelines only find no proven or expected advantage for carvedilol in presinusoidal disease'. Neither the reviewer nor I (web budget exhausted, fetch DNS failure) could open EASL 2025 or the Baveno VIII text, so the attribution is unverified, not refuted. The proposed line states the model's assumption ('its extra effect relaxes the sinusoids', scenario.js:49 sin: 0.8) as clinical fact, and it deletes the caveat that honestly flags it.
  - Source: Razafindrazoto CI et al., JGH Open, March 2022 (carvedilol vs propranolol for rebleeding in hepatosplenic schistosomiasis, n = 61): https://doaj.org/article/c5b01d101ffe456c9239dcaf837d7aed; EASL CPG on vascular diseases of the liver, J Hepatol 2026;84(2):399-456 (no carvedilol statement found in the excerpts reached); Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (full text not reachable)

### right-heart (`src/ui/decks/right-heart.js`)

A compact core deck with good engine use: a TR slider with the congestion lens, a web slider with the web and RA readouts, and the triphasic waveform with waves labelled. Its problems: - Telling constriction from heart failure is an objective, but the deck never teaches the discriminators. - The constriction ask's answer is oversimplified: about 18% of patients have a pericardium of normal thickness. - Two case cards repeat people from where-resistance: the Nepali man with an IVC web and the Hodgkin survivor with constriction. - The constriction case describes a swollen abdomen while the model holds only about 0.4 L of ascites. - The summary sentence wrongly says 'every pressure is high'. One addition would make the deck clinically sharper: a compare slide showing that a TIPS cannot decompress a liver congested by the heart, tied to EASL 2025's pre-TIPS cardiac work-up. The pulsatile portal vein and normal-HVPG ladder slides overlap the doppler, hvpg, sites and varices decks.

**Keep:** tr slide: whatIf 'Tricuspid leak' (tr 0 to 1) with lens 'heat' and the RHV Doppler. A good live demonstration of S-wave reversal and back-pressure.; web slide: whatIf on stenosis.IVCS_RA with sites ['web','ra'] and IVC Doppler. It shows the normal right atrium behind a high IVC well.; normal slide: waves: true on the RHV Doppler, with accurate notes on the a, S, v and D waves.; Summary built on the right atrial pressure as the discriminator between cardiac and post-hepatic causes.

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `constrict`
  - Now: ask: [..., 'Constrictive pericarditis: a thick pericardium on CT or MRI.']
  - Problem: The answer suggests a thick pericardium on imaging confirms the diagnosis. About 18% of surgically proven constriction has a pericardium of normal thickness. Echocardiography (septal shift, medial e' at or above 9 cm/s, expiratory hepatic vein diastolic reversal) and catheterization come first, and the deck's own notes say so.
  - Change: Answer: 'Constrictive pericarditis: echocardiography first (septal bounce, hepatic vein reversal that grows in expiration), then CT or MRI of the pericardium and catheterization; about 1 in 5 has a pericardium of normal thickness.'
  - Source: Talreja DR et al., Circulation 2003;108:1852-1857 (26 of 143 = 18% normal thickness); Welch TD et al., Circ Cardiovasc Imaging 2014;7:526-534 (Mayo echo criteria, PMID 24633783)

  - Problem: Objective 4 is 'Tell constriction and an IVC web from heart failure', but nothing on screen separates constriction from heart failure: both show a high RA and a normal HVPG. The discriminators sit only in the notes and are incomplete: - a normal-sized heart and ejection fraction; - the Kussmaul sign; - septal bounce; - expiratory, not inspiratory, hepatic vein reversal. The slide also shows LSM 15.0 kPa, a 'Borderline' stiffness, without saying it comes from congestion.
  - Change: line: as proposed. notes: 'Constriction follows viral or idiopathic pericarditis, cardiac surgery, radiotherapy or tuberculosis, and often first appears as ascites, before the legs swell, or as a stiff liver mistaken for cirrhosis. Both constriction and right heart failure raise the neck veins on inspiration (Kussmaul sign); constriction is told apart by a septum that shifts with breathing, a brisk mitral annular e\' and hepatic vein diastolic reversal that grows in expiration (in restrictive cardiomyopathy it grows in inspiration). Pericardiectomy treats it.' caveat: 'The model\'s Doppler does not show the breathing pattern of constriction.'
  - Source: Welch TD et al., Circ Cardiovasc Imaging 2014 (Mayo criteria: septal shift plus medial e' >= 9 cm/s or hepatic vein expiratory diastolic reversal ratio >= 0.79); engine run (constrict: ra 10.5, lsm 15.0, hvpg 1.1)

### outflow (`src/ui/decks/outflow.js`)

A solid advanced deck. It contrasts the two outflow lesions well: the HVPG fails with clotted hepatic veins and works in SOS. The caudate and DIPS slides use the engine well. The main gaps: - The SOS proof threshold says 'above 10 mmHg'; EBMT 2023 says 10 mmHg or more. - SOS prevention is out of date: EASL 2025 gives ursodeoxycholic acid for every HSCT, and defibrotide prophylaxis failed. - The causes list misses inotuzumab and gemtuzumab ozogamicin. - The summary table shows an HVPG of 0.3 mmHg for the DIPS row while all three hepatic veins are still clotted. - The case card's opening phrase is shared word for word with three other decks. - The first ask quizzes a different patient from the case card. - Five of nine lines break the one-sentence rule. The live model could do more: - a central-vein resistance slider on the SOS wedge; - the catheter monitor; - a days flow-time chart for recovery. All engine numbers below come from running the deck's own slide chain in the engine.

**Keep:** The caudate slide: CAUD 'note' mark with lens 'flow' shows why the caudate lobe keeps draining and enlarges; it is a clear, engine-driven explanation.; The HVPG fails with clotted hepatic veins (brackets hvpg 'misleads', ppg 'works') and works in SOS (cath 'result'). This contrast is the deck's core and is correct.; The open slide: one stented hepatic vein, a 60-day lapse, PV 26.1 to 9.7 mmHg and ascites 7.5 L to 0 L. A convincing picture of 'one open vein is enough'.; The recover lapse ramps fibrosis.R/L.post from 32 to 1, so SOS resolves smoothly (HVPG 11.3 to 2.5 mmHg).

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `soswedge`
  - Now: After a transplant, a gradient above 10 mmHg strongly supports the diagnosis. (line) / After a stem cell transplant an HVPG above 10 mmHg strongly supports the diagnosis. (notes)
  - Problem: EBMT 2023 counts an HVPG of 10 mmHg or more (not 'above 10') as proof of SOS, alongside histology. The number is also typed, against rule 6 (cut-offs go through an outlined pill).
  - Change: line: 'The hepatic veins are open and the resistance lies between the wedge and the free reading, so the HVPG rises to {hvpg}; after a transplant, an HVPG of {>=10 mmHg} or more confirms the diagnosis.' notes, second sentence: 'After a stem cell transplant an HVPG of 10 mmHg or more confirms the diagnosis, as a biopsy would.' Make the same 'above 10' to '10 mmHg or more' fix at lobule.js:55, sites.js:66 and hvpg.js:91.
  - Source: Mohty M et al., EBMT refined diagnostic and severity criteria for SOS/VOD in adults, Bone Marrow Transplant 2023 (doi 10.1038/s41409-023-01992-8; PMID 37095231): 'proven' = histology or HVPG >= 10 mmHg
- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `recover`
  - Now: Prevention matters: gentler conditioning where possible, and ursodeoxycholic acid in many centers.
  - Problem: 'In many centers' undersells the current guidance: - EASL 2025 recommends ursodeoxycholic acid prophylaxis for every HSCT. - EASL 2025 advises against defibrotide prophylaxis after the negative phase 3 HARMONY trial. The notes also give no defibrotide regimen.
  - Change: Replace the notes with: 'Mild and moderate disease resolves with careful fluid balance, diuretics and pain relief. Severe disease, with kidney or lung failure, carries a high mortality; defibrotide, 6.25 mg/kg IV every 6 hours for at least 21 days, is the treatment. For prevention, conditioning is made gentler where possible and every stem cell transplant gets ursodeoxycholic acid; defibrotide given as prophylaxis did not help in a phase 3 trial.'
  - Source: EASL CPG on vascular diseases of the liver, J Hepatol 2026;84:399-456 (online 2025), per Ground digest B (UDCA for all HSCT, LoE 1, strong; against defibrotide prophylaxis); HARMONY trial, Lancet Haematol 2023 (name and journal per digest B); Defitelio FDA label 2016 (dose)

  - Source: EASL CPG vascular diseases of the liver 2025 (stepwise approach, long-term anticoagulation; HCC surveillance in BCS), per digest B; AASLD vascular guidance, Northup PG et al., Hepatology 2021;73:366-413

### lobule (`src/ui/decks/lobule.js`)

Good cell biology (capillarization, stellate tone, zone 3), and case cards that match the model. But this is the fifth pass over the same tour of resistance sites: Sites, HVPG, Find the resistance, Presinusoidal PH and Outflow show schistosomiasis, cirrhosis, SOS and congestion in the same lobule views. Its 'wedge' slide repeats HVPG and Presinusoidal PH. One clinical error matters: the SOS slide shows an HVPG of 8.9 mmHg, while its notes say over 10 supports the diagnosis; Outflow shows 11.3 for the same disease. Statins appear as a resistance-lowering drug, although Baveno VIII withdrew support. Refocus on what only the lobule can teach: zone 3, and the sinusoid wall that sets the ascites protein. A compare-by-patient slide can show the latter, and the model already gets it right.

Render check: Item 14 is partly confirmed. The sinusoid view renders during a lapse; WebGL2 was available, and no fallback appeared. Placed after 'cirr' without its own patient, as specified, the lapse plays from the decompensated patient while the ramp starts at cirrhosis 0.6. The patient first improves (sinusoid 22.6 to 17.5 mmHg, ascites 1.1 to 0 L) and the protein rises to 2.5 'High' before ending at 1.9. The line says it falls. With preset 'cirr-comp' and params { cirrhosis: 0.6, albumin: 4 }, protein goes from '— No ascites' to 1.6 g/dL and ascites from 0 to 3.1 L. The chart is mistitled.

**Keep:** The 'zones' slide's oxygen and pressure lens buttons with the zone layers on: the only place the app shows why zone 3 suffers first.; The summary table with asc: true (SAAG and protein) and a 'Site in the lobule' note column, joining site, HVPG and ascites protein in one view.; Case cards that match the model. Schistosomiasis: large varices, spleen 16.9 cm, no ascites. Congestion: 5.5 L of ascites with protein 4.3 g/dL.; The schisto ask (schistosomiasis with hepatitis C cirrhosis: HVPG raised but below the true gradient), which tests understanding, not recall.; The sinusoid view during a lapse: fenestrae 'Closing 15%' and the lymph protein readout update live

### where-resistance (`src/ui/decks/where.js`)

The best retrieval-practice deck in the app. It pairs a case and a ladder with a reveal slide that carries Pressure / Pressure drop buttons, and the contrasts are well chosen: 2 against 5 by Doppler, 3 against 6 by the right atrium, 1 against 4 by history and protein. Every vignette matches the model's numbers. Three problems stand out. At 14 slides it breaks the 6-12 rule. Four patients are near copies of patients in other decks (hvpg, right-heart twice, ncph). Several notes speak to the instructor ('Give the room a minute', 'Tap Pressure drop'), and notes are printed in the learner handout. Cutting the ladders slide and making case 1 a single worked example brings it to 12 without losing a contrast. A second deck in the same format is the clearest new-deck idea in this area.

**Keep:** The predict-then-reveal pairs: the presenter's only real 'predict' step (docs/DESIGN_BLUEPRINT.md:944-950).; Pairs that differ by one reading: Doppler for cases 2 and 5, right atrial pressure for cases 3 and 6, history and protein for cases 1 and 4.; Lens buttons (LENS) on every answer slide: one tap shows where the pressure is lost, without touching the model.; The a6 teaching that the PPG misses a web because it subtracts the high IVC below it.

  - Change: (1) Delete 'ladders' and move its key sentence into the summary notes: 'Cases 2 and 5 share a ladder, and Doppler of the portal vein tells them apart; the web in case 6 sits between the IVC and the atrium, where neither gradient looks.' (2) Merge q1 and a1 into one worked example: { id: 'c1', preset: 'csph', cam: 'route', labels: ROUTE, vignette: (see where-2), compare: LENS, data: 'ladder', key: ['hvpg', 'ppg'], brackets: { hvpg: 'works', ppg: 'works' }, rail: true, kicker: 'Case 1', site: 'sin', title: 'Case 1, worked through: the resistance is in the sinusoids', line: 'Hepatitis C cirrhosis: the largest step falls between the wedged and free pressures, so the HVPG, {hvpg}, carries almost all of the PPG, {ppg}.', ask: a1's ask with the sites-8 answer }. In SIX, replace 'a1' with 'c1'. sections: [['Worked example', ['Case 1']], ['Cases 2 to 4', ['Case 2', 'Case 3', 'Case 4']], ['Cases 5 and 6', ['Case 5', 'Case 6']]]. That leaves 12 slides.

## Measuring


### doppler (`src/ui/decks/doppler.js`)

A solid core deck that sets out portal and hepatic Doppler by level of resistance, and most of its cases match the model: bc has tense ascites of 7.5 L, smv a 16.4 cm spleen with 76,000/µL platelets, rhf 5.5 L of ascites. Its weak points: - Clinical accuracy: the TIPS slide's "baseline in the first days" ignores covered-stent air and contradicts tips-doppler. - Hepatic vein wording: the waveform notes lump the tall a wave and S-wave reversal together. - Repeated cases: two cases nearly copy other decks' cases on the same preset (two-inflows/fugal, prehepatic/clot). - House rules: most lines run 2-3 sentences, and 5 notes run over 4 sentences. - Asks: most test recall. The biggest gap is a slide on imaging signs that prove CSPH. The engine has an unused paraumbilical (C3) Doppler probe, and the csph patient shows the point well: portal velocity is still a normal 24 cm/s while the reopened paraumbilical vein carries 15.6 cm/s of portal blood away from the liver. Cheap interactions also exist: a clot slider, a shunt-closure compare and a tricuspid regurgitation slider.

Render check: Item 3 is partly confirmed. The presenter never shows the pulsatility number at any size; only the pattern word appears. Explore mode's Doppler, however, prints 'Pulsatility 0.48' for a healthy portal vein next to 'Normal: toward the liver with gentle phasicity'. The deck's notes call 30% or more abnormal. Bands taken from VExUS would therefore call a healthy vein mildly abnormal. In cirrhosis the pattern word flickers between Phasic and Monophasic. Item 10: patient buttons on 'rhf' work on iPad, at 15 against 14 cm/s. The trace handover could not be judged at 1 to 3 fps; in-page captures showed a brief 'To-and-fro' reading.

**Keep:** The meal time-lapse with 'Fasting' and 'After a meal' words. The Doppler shows the fasting reading as a ghost (presenter.js:2019-2029), which gives a physiological reason for the scan-fasting rule.; The 'bc' and 'clot' slides as mirror images (no flow in the hepatic veins against no flow in the portal vein), and a summary table organized by where the resistance sits.; 'rhf' teaches the VExUS pulsatility fraction and explains why the HVPG stays normal in heart failure. Its case matches the model (RA 19 mmHg, ascites 5.5 L).; Live cut-off pills ({>=15 cm/s}, {<90 cm/s}) instead of typed numbers, and a lens on the slides where color is the point (velocity, direction, flow).; The heart-failure slide's live word 'Pulsatile', with a 15 cm/s mean and a −7 cm/s trough, makes the point at a glance; The healthy and cirrhotic velocities (28 against 14 cm/s) sit on either side of the 15 cm/s pill in the line

- **Addition, high, confirmed**, slide `new`
  - Now: (no slide on imaging signs that prove CSPH; probe C3 never used)
  - Problem: The deck says a normal Doppler does not exclude CSPH, but it never shows the ultrasound finding that proves it: a portosystemic collateral. In cACLD, collaterals on imaging are enough to diagnose CSPH. The paraumbilical Doppler probe exists (doppler.js:41) and no deck uses it. On the csph preset the model gives HVPG 11.6 mmHg, a normal portal velocity of 24.0 cm/s, C3 open at 15.6 cm/s portal to systemic, LSM 24.1 kPa and platelets 88,000/µL. That is a gray-zone stiffness patient whose Doppler settles the question (scratch run of the engine).
  - Change: Keep the slide fields as proposed (preset csph, cam 'wall', labels ['LPV'], C3 note mark, tool doppler C3, tiles ['hvpg','pvVel']). Change the vignette to: 'A 44-year-old woman with cirrhosis from autoimmune hepatitis, in remission on azathioprine, comes for her six-monthly liver ultrasound. She feels well, and her spleen is palpable.' (179 characters; the model's spleen is 15.6 cm). Change the ask to: ['The portal vein velocity is normal. Can she have CSPH?', 'Yes: the reopened paraumbilical vein is a portosystemic collateral, which is enough to diagnose CSPH, so she starts carvedilol.']. Keep the notes sentence on collaterals and cite Baveno VII/VIII and AASLD 2024; the exact Baveno VIII wording is unverified (no web access). Also retitle pvt-cirrhosis/base, for example 'A stiff liver on the way to a clot', or show its MPV, because the csph trunk reads 24 cm/s.
  - Source: de Franchis R et al., Baveno VII, J Hepatol 2022;76:959-974 (collaterals on imaging sufficient for CSPH; check the wording against Baveno VIII, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030); Kaplan DE et al., AASLD 2024, Hepatology 2024;79:1180-1211; engine: doppler.js:41, presenter-tools.js:38 (C3 title), REGIONS 'wall'
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `tips`
  - Now: Doppler is the surveillance test after TIPS: a baseline study in the first days, then when symptoms return.
  - Problem: Air trapped in the ePTFE covering can hide a new covered stent on ultrasound for days. Educational protocols therefore put the PTFE-stent baseline at 1 week to 1 month. EASL 2025 schedules follow-up visits at 4-6 weeks and then at 3, 6, 9 and 12 months, and suspects dysfunction if ascites has not cleared by 4-8 weeks. The same app says 'first weeks' in tips-doppler/stent (tips-doppler.js:58), so the two decks disagree.
  - Change: Use the notes change as written. Keep the follow-up wording general ('at follow-up visits'), and quote no interval until the EASL 2025 PDF has been read.

  - Problem: These notes lump two different signs together. Severe tricuspid regurgitation shrinks and then reverses the S wave. A high right atrial pressure without regurgitation gives a tall a wave and a more pulsatile trace. right-heart/tr (right-heart.js:30) teaches the split correctly, so the two decks disagree. The notes also run to 5 sentences.

  - Now: notes: 'A portal pulsatility fraction, (Vmax − Vmin)/Vmax, of 30% or more is abnormal, and 50% or more is severe.'

Presenter, desktop, phone and iPad: the stats (Max, Trough, Pulsatility, CI) are not displayed, because .dop-stats is display:none on phone and iPad and not rendered in the desktop card. Learners see only the pattern word. Healthy (probe, 2/13): 28 cm/s 'Phasic'. Cirr-decomp (slow, 5/13): 13-14 cm/s, flickering between 'Phasic' and 'Monophasic' because pi 0.05-0.20 straddles the 0.15 cut-off (doppler.js:297). RHF (10/13): 15 cm/s 'Pulsatile'. Hidden presenter values: healthy 0.44-0.49 (one sample 0.22), cirrhosis 0.05-0.20, RHF 1.23-1.43. Explore mode's Doppler shows them: healthy 'Pulsatility 0.48' with 'Normal: toward the liver with gentle phasicity.'; cirr-decomp 0.19-0.22 'Phasic'; RHF 1.22-1.40 'Pulsatile'. The reason is a 3 s swing over peak that includes breathing (doppler.js:244-255, 290). The engine's per-beat pvPI (engine.js:681-692) reads healthy 0.06-0.35, cirrhosis 0.06-0.11 and RHF 1.0-1.42. A VExUS-band rewording of the current number would print healthy 'mildly abnormal' (0.44-0.49), cirrhosis 'normal' and RHF 'severe'.

Then add the VExUS bands (under 30% normal, 30-49% mild, 50% or more severe) and hysteresis on the pattern word (for example Phasic above 0.18, Monophasic below 0.12). Keep the deck notes as written.

### stiffness (`src/ui/decks/stiffness.js`)

This is a well-built non-invasive-test deck: the rule-of-five scale, a meal lapse, and two good 'where it misleads' slides. It has two factual bugs. First, the 'rule' line calls the gray-zone patient 'the last case', but that row is the third; the last row sits at 28.6 kPa, already ruled in. Second, the 'year' ramp pushes the compensated patient into decompensation (4.7 L ascites, albumin 2.8 g/dL) on a slide about yearly FibroScans. The heart slide overstates congestion: in a 149-patient series the median at admission was about 12 kPa, not over 25. Baveno VIII items are missing or loose: the etiology limits on 25 kPa, '100 Hz' for spleen stiffness, the new spleen stiffness under 40 kPa that spares the scope, cACLD wording, and AASLD's US gray-zone rule. US radiology's shear-wave 'rule of four' is also missing. Several lines run to three sentences.

Render check: Render check of two items. Item 1 is confirmed and is the most serious defect found. The meal lapse plays from the CSPH patient of the 'spleen' slide, so on every size the FibroScan rises to 25.8 kPa and then falls to 14.8 kPa under the words 'After a meal'. Adding params { splanchnicTone: 1 } fixes it: 12.5 rises to 14.8 kPa. A scan of all 17 lapse slides found no other slide with this bug. Item 9: the patient buttons on 'schisto' work and ease in both directions, and the FibroScan follows them (24.1 against 6.6 kPa). On desktop, though, the extra button row folds the FibroScan picture behind 'Show'.

**Keep:** The meal lapse ('meal': days 8, splanchnicTone 1 to 0.72, from 'Fasting' to 'After a meal'): the stiffness climbs live from 12.5 to 14.8 kPa, close to the 15 kPa line, which is exactly why patients scan fasting.; The 'rule' scale with platelet sub-rows and the ask 'Stiffness 13 kPa, platelets 180. Is CSPH present, and is a scope needed?', which separates the two Baveno rules.; The honest caveat on 'fibrosis' ('The model's stiffness follows the gradient more closely than patients' does').; The pitfalls pair (schistosomiasis: LSM 6.6 kPa with PV 20.7 mmHg; heart failure: LSM 31.9 kPa with HVPG 0.6): strong, model-backed contrasts.; The schisto slide's contrast: FibroScan 6.6 kPa 'Normal' next to portal 20.7 mmHg 'Severe' and HVPG 3.7 mmHg 'Normal', all on one card; The FibroScan probe tag on the figure carries the reading while the instrument card changes

- **Accuracy, high, confirmed**, slide `rule`
  - Now: line: 'In compensated cirrhosis, 25 kPa or more means CSPH, and 15 or less with platelets of 150 or more rules it out. The last case, at {lsm@spleen} with an HVPG of {hvpg@spleen}, falls between: its low platelets point to CSPH.' of: [..., { preset: 'csph', name: 'Compensated, CSPH by HVPG' }, { preset: 'csph', params: { cirrhosis: 0.75 }, name: 'Compensated, CSPH' }]
  - Problem: {lsm@spleen} (24.1 kPa, HVPG 11.6) is the third row, not the last. The last row (csph with cirrhosis 0.75) computes to LSM 28.6 kPa, HVPG 16.8, platelets 88, so it is already ruled in. The row names don't help either: both are 'CSPH by HVPG'. '25 kPa or more means CSPH' leaves out the Baveno VIII limits (viral, alcohol-related or non-obese MASLD), and the rules apply to cACLD.
  - Change: line: 'In cACLD, 25 kPa or more rules CSPH in and 15 kPa or less with platelets of 150 or more rules it out; the third case, at {lsm@spleen} with an HVPG of {hvpg@spleen}, falls between, where its low platelets point to CSPH.' (40 words) Row renames as proposed.
  - Source: Numbers computed with createCore 'sequence' (src/worker-core.js) for both rows; Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030

A fall after the cause is treated predicts a lower risk of decompensation, and a rise means progression. Baveno VIII advises repeating the scan every year.'

I computed HVPG 17.8, LSM 30.8 kPa, platelets 54, varix 8.9 mm, ascites 4.7 L and albumin 2.8 g/dL, which is decompensated cirrhosis in 12 months. The slide is about a yearly FibroScan in compensated disease, and FibroScan often fails with that much ascites. Desktop tiles: 24.1 kPa 'Borderline' / HVPG 11.6 'CSPH' under 'Fasting → After a meal'. They rise to 24.6, 24.8 and 25.8 kPa 'CSPH' / 13.3 mmHg. A direct open (?slide=6) also plays the lapse, because lastAt = -1 makes lap true (presenter.js:1797); desktop read 25.0 kPa mid-lapse. - **Accuracy, medium, uncertain**, slide `heart`
  - Now: line: 'Readings above 25 kPa are common in decompensated heart failure and fall within days of diuresis. Check the jugular venous pressure and get an echocardiogram before calling it cirrhosis.' (the notes repeat 'readings over 25 kPa are common'); vignette: 'A 70-year-old woman with severe TR is admitted with heart failure and ascites. A FibroScan done for raised liver enzymes reads in the cirrhosis range.'
  - Problem: 'Above 25 kPa ... common' overstates it. In 149 patients with acute decompensated heart failure, the median stiffness was 12.2 kPa (IQR 6.3-23.6), falling to 8.7 kPa at discharge. Readings in the cirrhosis range occur with severe congestion, as in this case (RA 19 mmHg, LSM 31.9 kPa), but they are not the rule. The line also gives no live values. A minor point: VCTE often fails through ascites, and the case has ascites.
  - Change: Adopt the reviewer's line and notes, but drop the Soloveva numbers from any written source until verified. Keep 'and ascites' out of the vignette.
  - Why uncertain: I could not verify the Soloveva 2019 figures (median 12.2 kPa in 149 ADHF patients): search budget exhausted, fetch DNS fails. Smaller series (Colli 2010, Millonig 2010) show raised, reversible stiffness, but '>25 kPa common' is not something I can confirm or refute. The rewrite is safe either way, because it swaps an unsourced frequency claim for the model's own value (RA 19.0, LSM 31.9). The line loses its '(a value)' pill and gains {ra} and {lsm}, so tests/deck-pills.json needs UPDATE=1. Note that the model's rhf patient has 5.5 L ascites (grade 3), where VCTE usually fails.
  - Source: Soloveva A et al., J Card Fail 2019;25:176-187 (doi 10.1016/j.cardfail.2019.01.020); Colli A et al., Radiology 2010;257:872-878; Millonig G et al., J Hepatol 2010;52:206-210
- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `rule`
  - Now: notes: '... In the gray zone between, Baveno VIII uses a validated model (ANTICIPATE, from the stiffness and the platelets; with BMI in obese MASLD) and calls CSPH present at a probability of 75% or more. A spleen stiffness above 55 kPa also rules CSPH in. ...' (8 sentences)
  - Problem: (1) The 55 kPa spleen cut-off holds only on the 100 Hz VCTE probe. (2) Baveno VIII's new rule that a spleen stiffness under 40 kPa spares the screening endoscopy is missing. (3) Baveno VIII adds NICER as a third model. (4) Gray-zone patients are reassessed at 12 months. (5) US practice, under AASLD 2024, presumes CSPH at 20-25 kPa with platelets under 150, or 15-20 kPa with platelets under 110. (6) At 8 sentences, the notes break rule 11.
  - Change: Reviewer's notes, with the last sentence: 'A separate rule spares the scope in patients not on a beta-blocker: under 20 kPa with platelets of 150 or more, or a spleen stiffness of 40 kPa or less (check Baveno VIII's exact sign).'
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (rule-in criteria; spleen stiffness under 40 kPa, strong recommendation, via journal-page and Echosens excerpts: https://www.echosens.com/baveno-viii/); GI and Hepatology News, Sept 2026; Kaplan DE et al., AASLD 2024

### one-year (`src/ui/decks/one-year.js`)

This is a strong idea: one patient, one disease clock, and five instruments, closing on a talk trace. Three issues matter most. First, the carvedilol slide shows the varix tile halving at once (5.0 to 2.5 mm), because the model's varix size follows the gradient instantly. Second, the case is an obese patient with MASLD and diabetes, but the deck applies plain ANTICIPATE, ignores that the HVPG can underestimate in MASLD, and calls 8.8 mmHg 'not CSPH' without that caveat. Third, the carvedilol line says the trigger is 'before a first bleed or decompensation' rather than CSPH. By the house rules the deck ends on a trace, not a summary table. Day 0 shows the HVPG before the catheter slide reveals it. The follow-up story never mentions liver cancer surveillance every 6 months or treating the cause. There is no lapse, so the year jumps rather than plays.

Render check: Item 8 is refuted. d365 shows a catheter and ladder with no stiffness tile, and no later slide shows stiffness, so 'CSPH' at 25.75 kPa never appears; d0 and d90 read 'Borderline'. Item 13: with lapse { seconds: 8 } added, d90 eases well (FibroScan 17.8 to 21.3 kPa, platelets 159 to 114). The d180 scope grade flickers between None and F1, and once showed 'F2 Large, Red wale' at the end of the lapse while the tile read 3.7 mm Small. In both lapses the line shows the end values from the first frame.

**Keep:** One patient and one clock, with tick and when on each visit and the talk trace at the end: the only deck that follows a patient through every test.; The honest caveat on 'd180' ('Carvedilol would start by now; this case holds it back to show the pressure rising').; The 'd180' scope slide on varices meaning CSPH (Baveno), with a model varix of 3.7 mm (small), and the 'belly' slide's correct point that grade 1 ascites on imaging is not decompensation.; Carvedilol dosing in the notes (6.25 mg, then 12.5 mg a day), which matches AASLD 2024.; The d0 tiles (HVPG 8.8, LSM 17.8 'Borderline', platelets 159) set up the year well; The d90 FibroScan lapse: the reading climbs live beside a platelet tile that falls

## Complications


### varices (`src/ui/decks/varices.js`)

A sound anatomy-to-physiology deck that uses the engine well: the six-month lapse with the HVPG and varix chart, Laplace with the wall tool, and the heart-failure contrast. Five problems. (1) It has no summary table (rule 11). (2) The prophylaxis sentence on burst predates Baveno VIII's preference for carvedilol. (3) The scope-sparing rule omits the beta-blocker and spleen-stiffness arms. (4) Two slides repeat other decks almost word for word: fundal matches shunts/grs and svt matches prehepatic/svt, down to near-identical cases. (5) The eso slide breaks the no-repeated-pointer rule. Unused tools fit naturally: the paraumbilical Doppler (C3), the change-from-healthy lens for left-sided portal hypertension, a ladder with a right-atrium block for heart failure, and a carvedilol button beside the wall tool. All 9 lines run 2 or more sentences, and 2 notes exceed 4 sentences.

Render check: Item 5 is confirmed using a prototype with lens 'direction' and a C3 Doppler. The paraumbilical vein and the caput medusae are colored teal, which the key calls 'Usual', because C3 is defined from the left portal vein to the epigastric vein. The Doppler accepts C3 and reads 35 cm/s 'Portal → systemic', 'Open collateral', not the claimed 15.6 cm/s. The card's names disagree: 'PARAUMBILICAL VEIN' on desktop, 'UMBILICAL V.' on phone, and a footer 'Paraumb. C3' that shows the internal code.

**Keep:** The 'lapse' slide: 180 days of cirrhosis played live, with the HVPG and varix chart (flowtime, clock days) and the honest caveat that this takes years in patients.; The 'burst' slide: the Laplace equation with the wall tool, and the ask comparing Child–Pugh A without red signs against Child–Pugh C with red wale marks, which tests understanding rather than recall.; The 'nograd' slide's idea (no gradient, no collaterals), with Budd–Chiari as the contrast in the notes.; The 'rectal' ask on hemorrhoids against rectal varices, which corrects a common misconception.; The 'Paraumbilical vein' pointer and the caput drawn at the navel; The tiles 'Shunted 75% Large' and 'Liver flow 62% Low' tell the steal story

- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `burst`
  - Now: Large varices are treated with a non-selective beta-blocker or banding.
  - Problem: This misses who needs prophylaxis (small varices with red signs, or in Child–Pugh C, count too) and Baveno VIII and AASLD 2024's preference for carvedilol, which also lowers the risk of decompensation. Banding is for patients who cannot take a beta-blocker.
  - Change: As proposed. Also merge two of the other notes sentences to get to 4.
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030; Kaplan DE et al., AASLD Practice Guidance, Hepatology 2024;79:1180-1211; CALIBRE, Tripathi D et al., Aliment Pharmacol Ther 2025 (doi 10.1111/apt.70080)

  - Problem: Rule 11 asks for a closing visual 'table' summary under kicker 'Summary'. varices is the only deck with no summary at all, so the deck's main lesson (varices follow the portal-to-systemic gradient, except left-sided portal hypertension) is never put side by side.
  - Change: Use the proposed slide, with line: 'Varices form where the gradient drives blood: esophageal ones as the PPG climbs, fundal ones when a gastrorenal shunt or a splenic vein clot carries it to the fundus, and none in heart failure.' Add gv: 'Fundal varix' to COLS and gv: '↑ 2.5 to 5 mm, ↑↑ 5 mm or more' to CUT (table-marks.js:102, 104). Notes: 'collat' and 'lapse' are the same patient, so write 'Each row is a stage or a patient from this talk.'
  - Source: docs/deck-authoring-guide.md section 9 rule 11; src/ui/table-marks.js:102 and :104 (COLS and CUT have no gv); src/ui/presenter.js:2283 (header falls back to the raw key); slide states computed with createCore (src/worker-core.js:322-358) in local run states.mjs
- **Engine opportunity, medium, confirmed**, slide `umbilical`

  - Problem: The engine has a Doppler probe in the paraumbilical vein that no deck uses. C3', which exposes an internal edge id to learners.

### endoscopy (`src/ui/decks/endoscopy.js`)

The endoscopy deck covers screening, growth, grading, Laplace and the bleed in a tight 9 slides, and its first case matches the model exactly (platelets 159, LSM 12.5). Accuracy errors: - The 'large' notes still call beta-blockers with ascites an open question, against the new Baveno VIII statement. - 'tension' says bleeding 'needs' an HVPG of 12 mmHg. - 'band' lists incomplete pre-emptive TIPS criteria. - 'screen' omits the spleen stiffness route to skip endoscopy. - The summary line still says varix size decides treatment. Structure: - Of the 4 bleeding slides, 3 repeat variceal-bleed almost word for word, yet none shows the scope. - The engine's bleeding jet and balloon in the scope view go unused; I confirmed that a balloon stops a rupture in the model after 45 s. - The deck has no compare or whatIf. - 'large' loads a new patient with no case, so 'one patient' is not true.

Render check: Item 4 is confirmed. On the tension slide the wall card says the varix wall is 158% and 'Past tear', with a full dark-red bar, on desktop and phone. The varix is not bleeding, and spontaneous rupture is off in decks. The slide's own claim, 'Large, thin-walled varices are the ones that bleed', then contradicts what the instrument shows. The wording lives in instruments.js, not in the deck.

**Keep:** The 'screen' case matches the model exactly (platelets 159,000/µL, LSM 12.5 kPa) and teaches that the two Baveno bedside rules answer two different questions.; 'tension': Laplace with the varix-wall tool and an equation, tying size and red signs to wall tension.; 'bleed' teaches a counterintuitive point: portal pressure falls with lost volume. 'band' teaches that banding treats the varix, not the pressure, and the model shows HVPG rising after the bands.; The summary ask (carvedilol tolerated: is banding needed too?) matches Baveno VIII and AASLD 2024.; Laplace's equation with the live 7.7 mm token, and the wall cross-section showing T, r and w

- **Out of date, high, confirmed**, slide `large`
  - Now: Once ascites is present, Baveno VIII gives the beta-blocker for the varices; whether it also prevents further decompensation is still an open question.
  - Problem: Baveno VIII has a new statement: with ascites and varices, carvedilol or a conventional non-selective beta-blocker, carvedilol preferred, is recommended to prevent a first bleed and to lower ascites-related complications (LoE 3, strong, new, 81% agreement). The same statement sets stop rules. Calling the benefit 'an open question' is out of date. recompensation.js:49 has the same problem.
  - Change: Use as proposed, plus a US note: 'AASLD 2024 also allows carvedilol here, at no more than 12.5 mg a day, and stops it on the same signs.' Check this against the AASLD table. Fix recompensation.js:49 the same way.
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (journal-page extract; full text not read); CARVE-AS, Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121(6)
- **Accuracy, high, confirmed**, slide `tension`

A patient with a liver stiffness under 20 kPa and platelets of 150 or more can skip the screening endoscopy, ...
  - Problem: There are three problems: - Baveno VIII also spares the scope when spleen stiffness is under 40 kPa (LoE 1, strong, modified, 100% per extract). No deck says so. - The second sentence repeats the first. - Platelets are written without units.
