### variceal-bleed (`src/ui/decks/variceal-bleed.js`)

The best of the four decks. It follows one live bleed beat by beat, shows shock before anemia, and is up to date with Baveno VIII on pre-emptive TIPS (including an HVPG of 20 mmHg or more and the late window), restrictive transfusion, erythromycin and the short vasoactive course. Fixes needed: (1) the endoscopy timing ('once the blood pressure holds' leaves out 'sooner if unstable'); (2) the ceftriaxone duration (5 days should be up to 7); (3) the stent-first bridge, which does not fit US practice; (4) an inflated octreotide-in-the-model claim; (5) a raw 'hb' table header. Notes run 6 to 7 sentences (oct 143 words, blood 145). The never-used balloon-tamponade engine cannot be added here, because after 4 bands the chain cannot rebleed (a second tear loses 13 mL). It belongs in a companion deck (see 'new deck: refractory-bleed').

**Keep:** The beat-by-beat trace kept on for the whole talk, with tiles that show the heart rate rising and the MAP falling while the hemoglobin stays at 13.6 g/dL.; The 'minutes' ask (a hemoglobin of 13.5 g/dL with a pulse of 140) and the 'tear' ask (a man on carvedilol with a pulse of 88): applied, not recall.; The 'tips' slide: Child–Pugh worked out from typed labs, with the Baveno VIII criteria as causes, including an HVPG of 20 mmHg or more.; The 'blood' slide explains restrictive transfusion through portal pressure, which the model actually shows (the PV rises as volume returns).

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `band`
  - Now: Endoscopy within 12 hours, once the blood pressure holds. Banding does not lower portal pressure; the HVPG rises slightly as the varix closes. If bleeding continues, an esophageal stent or a balloon tube bridges to a rescue TIPS.
  - Problem: Baveno VIII says endoscopy within 12 hours of presentation, and as soon as possible when the patient stays unstable. 'Once the blood pressure holds' can be read as waiting for stability in a patient who will not stabilize. The line also packs three ideas into three sentences. The banding point is the treatment deck's lesson, and the bridge belongs to refractory bleeding.
  - Change: Line: 'Banding at an endoscopy within 12 hours, once resuscitation is under way, and sooner if the patient stays unstable, stops the bleeding.' Make the same fix at endoscopy.js:64 and :71.
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (endoscopy timing, LoE 2, strong); Kaplan DE et al., AASLD 2024
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `oct`
  - Now: Give ceftriaxone 1 g a day as well, for up to 5 days (AASLD stops it at discharge if that comes sooner)
  - Problem: AASLD, EASL and Baveno all give ceftriaxone 1 g a day for up to 7 days, not 5. Baveno VIII's shorter course, or none, in Child–Pugh A is a European weak recommendation that AASLD does not make. Under the house rule (AASLD where US practice differs), say so. The notes run 7 sentences.
  - Change: As proposed. Keep 'Outside the US, terlipressin or somatostatin are used in its place' only if it fits within 4 sentences.
  - Source: Kaplan DE et al., AASLD 2024; EASL CPG on decompensated cirrhosis, J Hepatol 2018;69:406-460; Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (antibiotics LoE 2, weak; erythromycin LoE 2, weak)
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `band`
  - Now: If bleeding cannot be controlled, a covered self-expanding esophageal stent (preferred over a balloon tube where available) buys time as a bridge to a rescue TIPS. / ask answer: 'A covered esophageal stent (preferred) or a balloon tube, as a bridge to a rescue TIPS.'
  - Problem: The stent's advantage rests on one 28-patient trial (success 66% against 20%, with no difference in 6-week survival). In the US the dedicated variceal stent is largely unavailable, so the bridge is a balloon tube, held for no more than about 24 hours with the airway protected. The deck is written for a US audience.
  - Change: Notes: 'If bleeding cannot be controlled, a balloon tube (Sengstaken–Blakemore or Minnesota), placed after intubation, holds for up to 24 hours as a bridge to a rescue TIPS; where a covered esophageal stent is available, it controls bleeding as well with fewer complications.' Ask answer: 'A balloon tube for up to 24 hours, or a covered esophageal stent where available, as a bridge to a rescue TIPS.'
  - Source: Escorsell A et al., Hepatology 2016;63:1957-1967; Baveno VII, J Hepatol 2022;76:959-974; US availability is clinical knowledge, not a guideline statement
- **Engine opportunity, medium, confirmed**, slide `summary`
  - Now: visual: 'table', cols: ['hr', 'map', 'hb', 'pv', 'ppg']
  - Problem: COLS has no 'hb' entry, so the table heads that column with the raw key 'hb', and its heading has no cut-off tooltip (CUT has no hb either).
  - Change: In table-marks.js add hb: 'Hb' to COLS, hb: '↓ under 12, ↓↓ under 7 g/dL' to CUT, 'hb' to UP_GOOD, and hb: 0.5 to STEP_REL. Add gv to COLS/CUT at the same time.
  - Source: src/ui/table-marks.js:102, 104; src/ui/presenter.js:2283 (cols.map((k) => cutTh(k, COLS[k] || k, CUT[k])))
- **Accuracy, low, confirmed**, slide `blood`
  - Now: In cirrhosis saline adds sodium, so a balanced crystalloid, given with care, is a common choice.
  - Problem: The reasoning does not hold, because balanced crystalloids carry sodium too (lactated Ringer's has 130 mmol/L). The guidelines name no preferred crystalloid. The notes run 6 sentences and 145 words.
  - Change: Replace with: 'Crystalloid is given sparingly: its sodium and water end up as ascites.' Also cut one other sentence, so the notes come to 4.
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (volume to restore hemodynamic stability; no fluid named, per the excerpts); Villanueva C et al., NEJM 2013;368:11-21
- **Accuracy, low, uncertain**, slide `oct`
  - Now: Start octreotide, a 50 µg bolus then 50 µg an hour (also checks[1], treatment/oct notes, endoscopy/oct notes)
  - Problem: Digest A reports that the AASLD 2024 table gives the octreotide infusion as 25 to 50 µg an hour. The 2017 AASLD guidance and the AGA's 50 µg an hour is what the decks quote. I could not open the 2024 table to settle it.
  - Change: Check Hepatology 2024;79:1180-1211, Table 3. If it says '25 to 50', the safest wording is 'a 50 µg bolus, then 50 µg an hour (25 to 50 in AASLD 2024)', applied in variceal-bleed (line, notes, checks[1]), treatment/oct and endoscopy/oct together.
  - Why uncertain: Both digests report 25 to 50 µg an hour in AASLD 2024, likely from the same extract. AASLD 2017 and common US practice give 50 µg an hour. I could not open the 2024 table (search budget exhausted, fetch DNS blocked). The conditional advice to change all decks together, or none, is sound.
  - Source: Kaplan DE et al., AASLD 2024 (not verified here); AGA Clinical Practice Update, Gastroenterology 2024 (PMID 37978969)
- **Engine opportunity, low, confirmed**, slide `oct`
  - Now: The portal pressure here falls with the blood loss too; in the model octreotide adds about 1 mmHg of that fall.
  - Problem: I re-ran the chain with the same bleeding time but without octreotide. Octreotide's share of the fall is about 0.4 mmHg on 'oct' (PV 15.1 against 15.5 mmHg) and about 0.6 mmHg by 'band' (17.9 against 18.5). A second engine effect is worth knowing for this deck. A slide with params settles twice (worker-core.js:336 and :352). The open bleed keeps running through both settles (engine.js:456), while clotting runs only off the quiet clock (engine.js:570). So 'oct' bleeds about 5 model minutes longer than a slide without params, and most of its MAP fall (72 against 79 mmHg with no params) comes from that extra time, not from the drug.
  - Change: Notes: 'in the model octreotide adds less than 1 mmHg to that fall'.
  - Source: src/worker-core.js:336-337, 352; src/engine/engine.js:452-458, 570, 664-668; my runs local run bleed.mjs
- **Teaching, low, confirmed**, slide `tips`
  - Now: ask: ['Which patients get a pre-emptive TIPS after a variceal bleed?', 'Child–Pugh C 10 to 13, or Child–Pugh B over 7 with active bleeding at endoscopy.'] / notes: 'A patient who misses the 72-hour window can still benefit from a TIPS placed later...'
  - Problem: The ask is recall, and its answer leaves out the HVPG criterion that the causes list shows. The late window is Baveno VIII's weak, LoE 4 statement, so 'can still benefit' is too firm.
  - Change: Ask: ['A Child–Pugh B8 patient's varix is no longer bleeding when the scope reaches it. Does he get a pre-emptive TIPS?', 'Not on Child–Pugh alone: Child–Pugh B needs more than 7 points with active bleeding at endoscopy. An HVPG of 20 mmHg or more, where measured, would qualify him.']. Notes: 'may still benefit from a TIPS placed later (a weak recommendation on limited evidence)'.
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (pre-emptive TIPS LoE 1, strong, 93%; late window LoE 4, weak, 88%)
- **Humanizer, low, confirmed**, slide `tear`
  - Now: tear: 'Blood pours into the esophagus. The heart rate usually rises first, unless a beta-blocker blunts it. Blood pressure is still normal at this stage.' / oct: 'Start octreotide, a 50 µg bolus then 50 µg an hour, with ceftriaxone 1 g a day. The first unit and a liter of fluid run in, but the bleed still outpaces them and the blood pressure falls to {map}.' / blood: 'A second unit brings the blood pressure up to {map}. The hemoglobin has barely moved, because it lags the bleed. Once the circulation is stable, transfuse only below 7 g/dL, to a target of 7 to 8.'
  - Problem: Each line runs 2 to 3 sentences against the one-sentence rule. On 'blood', the transfusion threshold is a rule and should be an outlined cut-off pill.

### ascites (`src/ui/decks/ascites.js`)

A strong mechanism deck: Starling, the lymph route, a live lapse, then the tap. Its clinical and engine content is sound. One clinical error needs fixing: low ascitic protein alone does not justify SBP prophylaxis. Two other problems: 9 of 11 lines run to two sentences, and 'start' and 'lymph' ask the same question. Structure: the deck ends on the SAAG-protein quadrant, not a summary table. The quadrant also leaves out the two patients (PVT and schistosomiasis) who carry objective 3. A five-row table of sinusoidal pressure, ascites, SAAG and protein would close the deck. The 60-day lapse squeezes CSPH-to-grade-3 into two months; recompensation runs the same ramp over a year. The deck has no live control at all. A 'Heart failure' patient button on the Budd-Chiari slide, with an RA tile, would make the JVP point visible. Model numbers were checked with a scratch run of src/worker-core.js. The web-search budget ran out mid-review; items marked medium rest on the shared digests or memory.

Render check: Item 11 is confirmed. With patient buttons on 'bc', the right atrial pressure moves from 3.3 to 19.0 mmHg and back, with intermediate values both ways. SAAG (1.4) and protein (4.3) rightly stay the same, so the RA alone separates the two patients, which is good teaching. But the slide's red 'Clot in hepatic veins' mark and the glow on the hepatic veins stay on the heart-failure patient. The desktop figure also names the right atrium twice.

**Keep:** 'walls' (visual walls) with the reflection-coefficient notes: the clearest explanation in the app of why sinusoids, not gut capillaries, make ascites.; 'fill' time-lapse with the flowtime chart (lines sin and asc) and an honest kidney caveat.; 'pvt' and 'presin' put {pv} and {sin} side by side, so the learner sees high portal pressure with normal sinusoids and no ascites.; The varied, model-consistent case cards (MASH woman, protein C deficiency, Yemen, postpartum Budd-Chiari).; Putting saag, tp and ra side by side: identical SAAG and protein, with only the RA differing between Budd–Chiari and heart failure

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `lowprot`
  - Now: notes: 'A protein below 1.5 g/dL means weak opsonic activity and a higher risk of spontaneous bacterial peritonitis, so prophylactic antibiotics are considered.' ask answer: 'Weak opsonic activity: a higher risk of spontaneous bacterial peritonitis, so prophylaxis is considered.'
  - Problem: Low ascitic protein alone is not an indication for primary SBP prophylaxis. AASLD and EASL require low protein plus kidney dysfunction or liver failure. The slide teaches the wrong indication, and its tp tile reads 1.4 g/dL with the label 'SBP risk'.
  - Change: lowprot notes (4 sentences): 'In cirrhosis the sinusoids capillarize: the fenestrae close and a basement membrane and collagen form in the space of Disse, so the lymph and the ascites are thin, with a total protein below 2.5 g/dL. Diuretics concentrate the fluid and raise its protein somewhat. A protein below 1.5 g/dL means weak opsonic activity and a higher risk of spontaneous bacterial peritonitis. Primary prophylaxis is given only when kidney dysfunction (creatinine 1.2 mg/dL or more, BUN 25 mg/dL or more, or sodium 130 mmol/L or less) or liver failure (Child–Pugh 9 or more with bilirubin 3 mg/dL or more) comes with it; in the US with ciprofloxacin or trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole.' Ask answer as the review proposed.
  - Source: Biggins SW et al., AASLD Practice Guidance, Hepatology 2021;74:1014-1048; EASL CPG decompensated cirrhosis, J Hepatol 2018;69:406-460; Fernandez J et al., Gastroenterology 2007 (primary prophylaxis criteria)

  - Problem: The slide takes the patient from CSPH (cirrhosis 0.6) to grade 3 ascites (5.2 L in the model) in two months. Decompensation in CSPH develops over years. recompensation/ascites (recompensation.js:31) runs the identical ramp over 365 days, so the two decks give the same disease different time courses.
  - Change: As proposed: days: 365, lapse: { seconds: 9, from: 'CSPH', to: 'A year on' }, title 'Ascites builds as the cirrhosis advances', notes 'over a year'. Then update the ascites-6 table numbers (fill row: sinusoids 24.4 mmHg, ascites 4.5 L).

A value of 1.1 g/dL or more indicates portal hypertension.' | bc: '...so the SAAG is high and protein is 2.5 g/dL or more.'

Same idea for presin's line: 'Resistance in the portal tracts leaves the sinusoids only mildly raised, at {sin}, so ascites is uncommon despite high portal pressure.'

Add to bc notes: 'Against the heart-failure patient, the fluid is the same and the right atrial pressure is not.' If a compare is still wanted, remove or reword the mark first.

### tap (`src/ui/decks/tap.js`)

Teaching one patient through tap, albumin, return, diuretics and TIPS is right, and the abdomen tool makes it concrete. Four problems stand out. The first slide breaks the case-card rule (ramp). The albumin slide shows a MAP change of 0 while its line says albumin refills the circulation, and the obvious fix (infuse albumin) makes the model's ascites return faster. The patient responds well to diuretics and then gets a TIPS 'for refractory ascites'. The closing 'heart' slide repeats ascites/hf, breaks the one-patient story and duplicates a check. On currency: long-term albumin, the diuretic US/EU framing and HRS-AKI need updating, and SBP and AKI asks should test management, not recall. Seven of nine lines need one-sentence rewrites. Cutting 'heart' brings the deck to 8 slides, or 9 with an optional 'ascites returns on diuretics' slide.

**Keep:** The one-patient arc with tool abdomen and delta tiles on every step.; lvp, the only use of the PICD event (paracentesis without albumin over 5 L, engine.js:953-964).; The 'back' ask that separates ascites off diuretics from refractory ascites.; The summary table with vs 'first' and the line 'The tap empties the abdomen, but only diuretics or TIPS keep the fluid from returning.'

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `diuretics`
  - Now: notes: 'Spironolactone 100 mg a day, with furosemide 40 mg for recurrent ascites; outside the US, EASL starts a first episode on spironolactone alone...' (6 sentences); line: 'Spironolactone with furosemide, and less salt. ...'; ask: ['Starting doses of spironolactone and furosemide?', ...]
  - Problem: The notes read as though furosemide is added only for recurrent ascites, which is the EASL rule; AASLD starts both drugs together. The notes run to 6 sentences, against the rule of 2 to 4. The line opens with a fragment. The ask is a fragment and the same dose recall as recompensation/diuretics.
  - Change: line: 'Spironolactone with furosemide and a 2 g sodium diet make the kidneys excrete sodium, and water follows: the abdomen is down to {asc}.' notes: 'AASLD starts spironolactone 100 mg with furosemide 40 mg a day; EASL starts a first episode on spironolactone alone and adds furosemide for recurrent ascites, a slow response or a high potassium. Both rise in a 100:40 ratio to at most 400 and 160 mg, with about 2 g of sodium (5 g of salt) a day and no fluid restriction unless the sodium is below 125 mmol/L. Aim to lose up to 0.5 kg a day without leg edema, or 1 kg with it. Stop or reduce for kidney injury, a sodium below 125 mmol/L, encephalopathy or disabling cramps.' ask: ['After two weeks on diuretics his weight has not changed, yet his spot urine sodium-to-potassium ratio is 2.5. What is going on?', 'His kidneys are excreting enough sodium (a ratio above 1 means about 78 mmol a day or more), so he is eating more salt than prescribed. Review the diet before raising the doses.']
  - Source: AASLD 2021, Hepatology 2021;74:1014-1048 (combination start; spot urine Na:K); EASL CPG 2018 (spironolactone alone for a first episode)
- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `summary`
  - Now: notes: '...long-term albumin is used in some centers for decompensated cirrhosis.'; of: [... { id: 'albumin', kicker: 'Tap', title: 'With albumin' } ...]
  - Problem: The albumin sentence is out of date. PRECIOSA (2025) missed its primary endpoint, ATTIRE was negative, and ANSWER is a single open trial. Neither AASLD nor EASL recommends routine long-term albumin. The 'With albumin' row repeats the 'Paracentesis' row exactly (0 L, PPG 17.7, liver flow 61%), because COLS cannot show serum albumin.
  - Change: notes: 'The tap clears the fluid at once but leaves its cause; albumin protects the circulation; diuretics clear it more slowly and keep it away; TIPS clears it by lowering the sinusoidal pressure, at the cost of the liver's portal blood. Refer anyone with refractory ascites for transplant assessment, and avoid NSAIDs, ACE inhibitors and ARBs. Weekly albumin improved survival in one open trial, but PRECIOSA missed its primary endpoint, so long-term albumin is not standard care. In the US an implanted pump that moves ascites to the bladder (alfapump) was approved in 2024 for recurrent or refractory ascites.' Remove the 'albumin' row from of.
  - Source: Caraceni P et al., Lancet 2018 (ANSWER); China L et al., NEJM 2021;384:808-817 (ATTIRE); PRECIOSA, J Hepatol 2025;82:S10 LBO-003; FDA PMA alfapump Dec 2024 (via Healio 2025); src/ui/table-marks.js COLS (no salb)

### spleen (`src/ui/decks/spleen.js`)

This is a short, clear deck with good engine use: a growth lapse, a splenic vein Doppler with a ghost after embolization, and a velocity lens after splenectomy. The main problem is the opening story. A healthy MASLD case (a case card on a healthy reference slide, which section 14 forbids) turns into cirrhosis with CSPH in two years, and the line calls this 'two years of cirrhosis'. The 'screen' slide leaves out Baveno VIII's new spleen-specific rule, spleen stiffness under 40 kPa, although this is the spleen deck. The model's platelet rise after embolization (+16% at 6 months) is much smaller than in patients, where the count usually doubles, and no caveat says so. Splenectomy needs the vaccination point. Two lines run to three sentences.

Render check: Item 12: the recommendation is not ready. Live splenicRx buttons on 'pse' change the numbers, but platelets go from 96 to 380 'Normal' in a single quick count, and the spleen tile reads '0.0 cm Normal'. On the figure the spleen does not fade: its inline opacity changes, but the organ raster is not redrawn, because its key has no spleen opacity. A red circled cross is drawn over a spleen that is still whole. 'Embolization' changes neither platelets nor spleen size acutely. On phone the slide's card shows the Doppler only, so the platelet change is not visible at all.

**Keep:** The 'grown' lapse with spleen, platelet and PV tiles against the normal spleen: growth and pooling in one live picture.; The Doppler of the splenic vein (SV_CONF) on 'pse' with a ghost of the pre-embolization trace: the only use of SV_CONF in the measuring decks, and exactly the right vessel.; The splenectomy slide's velocity lens showing the splenic vein going still, tied to the clot risk.; The summary ask on thrombopoietin receptor agonists (avatrombopag or lusutrombopag about 10 days before a high-risk procedure; no routine correction for low-risk ones): accurate and practical.; The embolization infarct patches and the splenectomy cross are clear symbols on the figure; The deck's own 'splenectomy' slide shows the faded spleen properly, because it arrives as a new state

### pvt-cirrhosis (`src/ui/decks/pvt-cirrhosis.js`)

The deck is well built. It has a clot slider, a Doppler ghost, the gradients slide with brackets, a 180-day anticoagulation lapse and a talk trace, and it teaches HVPG against PPG clearly. Its management story is behind Baveno VIII: - The case has CSPH (HVPG 11.6), which by itself calls for a beta-blocker (carvedilol preferred) and makes a screening endoscopy unnecessary. The deck instead bands four times before anticoagulating, against its own notes and against 'do not delay'. - The first slide teaches slow portal flow while the model shows a normal 24 cm/s on the Doppler. - The anticoagulation indications drop the '50% or more' and 'under 6 months' qualifiers. - The model's clot raises the PV by 4.6 mmHg at once; Nery 2015 found partial PVT not independently linked to progression, so the clot slide needs a caveat. All fixes keep the slide count in range (7 to 8 slides).

Render check: Item 6 is confirmed on desktop and phone. The 'Slow flow toward a stiff liver' slide shows a main portal vein at 24 cm/s 'Hepatopetal'. By the card's own 15-40 cm/s range and the pvVel tile cut-off of under 15, that velocity is normal, so the title's key claim is not visible. The causes list also includes 'A high INR does not protect', which is not a risk factor.

**Keep:** clot slide: whatIf on thrombus.PV_TRUNK with a Doppler ghost from 'base' (tool delta) shows the jet through the narrowed vein (pvVel 24 to 41 cm/s).; gradients slide: lens 'drop', PPG equation in the fixed form, and brackets hvpg 'misleads', ppg 'works'. It shows HVPG 11.0 against PPG 18.5 mmHg, a model teaching moment.; anticoag lapse over 180 days plus the 'course' talk trace, where the PV-WHVP gap opens and closes. A rare good use of the talk trace.; base case card agrees with the model (platelets 88,000/µL, compensated, no ascites).; The line's point about the INR and rebalanced hemostasis; The Doppler as the slide's instrument, since slow flow is the risk factor

- **Out of date, high, uncertain**, slide `scope, band (replace both)`
  - Now: scope line: 'Give bleeding prophylaxis before the anticoagulant, but do not let the endoscopy delay it.' / band: action [BAND, BAND, BAND, BAND] / band notes: 'Banding just before anticoagulation leaves ulcers that can bleed on it, so a beta-blocker, if tolerated, avoids that.'
  - Problem: Three things go wrong here: - The case has CSPH (HVPG 11.6 mmHg). Under Baveno VIII that alone calls for a non-selective beta-blocker, carvedilol preferred, and a patient on one needs no screening endoscopy. - The deck runs four banding sessions, weeks of work in practice, before anticoagulating. That contradicts 'do not delay' and the deck's own note that a beta-blocker avoids post-banding ulcers. - It also contradicts the beta-blockers, one-year and stiffness decks.
  - Change: If adopted: use the reviewer's 'carv' slide, but with compare [{ label: 'No beta-blocker', params: { drugs: { carvedilol: false } } }, { label: 'Carvedilol', params: { drugs: { carvedilol: true } }, own: true }]. Make the ask 'Must anticoagulation wait for an endoscopy?' with the answer 'No: he takes carvedilol for his CSPH, and the varices must never delay anticoagulation.' Then retitle 'course' 'The gap between portal and wedged pressure followed the clot', and line: 'The gap between the portal and wedged pressure lines is the clot; recanalization is checked by imaging about every three months, not by the HVPG.' Remove calm: ['hvpg'] from the summary, retitle the summary row 'On carvedilol', and change objective 3 and check 3 as proposed.
  - Why uncertain: The engine numbers are right: carv gives PV 19.5, HVPG 8.6, varix 4.95 and MAP 90.4, and after the 180-day anticoagulation PV 15.0, PPG 10.9 and varix 2.0 (my run). The Baveno VIII points (CSPH calls for an NSBB, carvedilol preferred; no screening endoscopy on one) match the digests. Several things weaken the finding. (1) It misreads the deck: action [BAND x4] is four bands in one session, since each band action adds one band up to a cap of 4 (engine.js:948-949). It is not 'four banding sessions, weeks of work'. (2) Whether the 'no endoscopy on an NSBB' rule replaces pre-anticoagulation variceal assessment in PVT is not confirmed; the Baveno VIII vascular statement found says only 'do not delay anticoagulation for endoscopic prophylaxis'. (3) It misses the knock-on effects. Carvedilol drops the HVPG from 11.6 to 8.6 at the new step, so 'course' ('Portal pressure followed the clot while HVPG stayed flat', pvt-cirrhosis.js:71), the summary's calm: ['hvpg'] and its line would become false, and the talk trace would show the HVPG falling. (4) Observational meta-analyses link NSBB use to new PVT through slower portal flow (low confidence, from memory). This sits awkwardly beside the deck's 'slow flow' first slide, though no guideline advises against the NSBB.
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026 (doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030): NSBB, particularly carvedilol, in cACLD with CSPH; no screening endoscopy on an NSBB; vascular panel 'do not delay anticoagulation for endoscopic prophylaxis' (per digests A and B); engine run locally (steps.mjs)
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `anticoag`
  - Now: Baveno VIII advises anticoagulation for transplant candidates with any portal vein clot, and considers it for a recent partial or complete clot of the main portal vein and for any clot that causes symptoms.
  - Problem: The notes drop the qualifiers that define the indication: a recent clot (under 6 months) that fills 50% or more of the trunk. As written, every partial clot qualifies. They also omit: - treating a smaller clot only if it grows on follow-up; - not anticoagulating an established cavernoma; - holding treatment during active variceal bleeding (Baveno VIII vascular panel). Baveno VIII says unrevised earlier statements stay valid, so the attribution can stand. The specific PVT wording in Baveno VIII is not verified.
  - Change: notes: 'Anticoagulation is advised for transplant candidates with any portal vein clot, for a recent clot (under 6 months) that fills more than half of the portal vein, with or without extension into the superior mesenteric vein, and for a clot that causes symptoms; a smaller clot is treated if it grows on follow-up, and an old occlusion that has formed a cavernoma is not. The vein reopens more often when treatment starts early. It continues for at least six months, and until transplant in candidates, and is held during active variceal bleeding; the clot can recur after it stops. Many partial clots also clear without treatment, and whether one worsens the outlook is uncertain.' line as proposed.
  - Source: Baveno VII statements on PVT carried into Baveno VIII (J Hepatol 2026 abstract: unrevised recommendations remain valid); EASL CPG vascular diseases of the liver, J Hepatol 2026;84:399-456; AGA Clinical Practice Update on PVT in cirrhosis, Gastroenterology 2025 (PMID 39708000); Loffredo L et al., Gastroenterology 2017 (71% vs 42%)

  - Problem: The deck covers anticoagulation but not what happens when the vein does not reopen or the patient bleeds. Baveno VIII made a modified statement on this: manage acute variceal bleeding as without PVT, and consider TIPS with or without portal vein recanalization with ascites, a growing clot or transplant candidacy.
  - Change: Append to the notes: 'If a variceal bleed occurs, it is treated as in any patient with cirrhosis, and a TIPS, with the portal vein reopened if needed, is considered when there is ascites, a growing clot or a transplant ahead.' Optional new slide, if the deck has room: preset 'csph' with params { thrombus: { PV_TRUNK: 0.6 }, tips: { on: true } }. In the model the PV stays at 19.5 mmHg with PPG 15.0 behind the clot while the HVPG falls to 5.4. That shows why the trunk must be reopened as well.
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (LoE 5, strong, modified, 85% agreement), per digest B; engine run (clot + TIPS: pv 19.54, ppg 15.0, hvpg 5.4)

  - Change: causes: ['Portal velocity under 15 cm/s', 'More advanced liver disease', 'Splenectomy or other abdominal surgery']

### recompensation (`src/ui/decks/recompensation.js`)

A well-built time-line deck: talk trace, ticks, a Baveno VIII criteria list, carvedilol compare buttons. One clinical problem: the carvedilol slide says beta-blockers in ascites prevent only bleeding and that further benefit 'is still open'. Baveno VIII now recommends a beta-blocker, carvedilol preferred, with ascites and varices, and the beta-blockers deck already says so. A subtle model point goes untaught. The HVPG at 'cure' and in the summary (9.3 mmHg, rated Mild) is measured on carvedilol; off the drug it is 12.2 mmHg. A learner reading the table could think CSPH is gone and stop carvedilol. A 'keep' slide with on/off buttons fixes this. A 'Kept drinking' summary row would show the alternative course. The asks repeat 'abstinence beats drugs' four times and copy tap's dose question. They could test the new Baveno VIII nuances instead (imaging-only ascites, no screening endoscopy on an NSBB).

**Keep:** The talk trace 'Two years on one chart' with tick labels at each time point.; causesHead 'Baveno VIII criteria' with the three criteria as causes, matching the >6-month rule.; The carv slide's compare buttons (No beta-blocker / Carvedilol) with the ladder.; The closing message that recompensation is not a cure and surveillance continues.

  - Problem: Baveno VIII adds a new statement: with ascites and varices, carvedilol or a conventional NSBB, carvedilol preferred, prevents a first bleed and lowers ascites-related complications. The deck's wording predates this and contradicts beta-blockers.js:81 ('It also prefers carvedilol with ascites and varices'). 'the kidneys fail' is vague, and the line is two sentences.
  - Change: line: 'Carvedilol lowers the HVPG to {hvpg}; with ascites, reduce or stop it if the systolic pressure stays under {<90 mmHg} or acute kidney injury develops.' notes: 'Baveno VIII now recommends a beta-blocker, carvedilol preferred, for patients with ascites and varices, to prevent a first bleed and lower ascites-related complications such as kidney injury. His varices are large, so carvedilol is indicated anyway, at no more than 12.5 mg a day. Reduce or stop it when the systolic pressure stays below 90 mmHg, the mean below 65 mmHg, or HRS-AKI develops, and restart when these resolve.' Make the same change at endoscopy.js:47.
  - Source: Baveno VIII Faculty, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030 (new statement, LoE 3, strong, 81%, per journal-page extracts); CARVE-AS, Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121(6); src/ui/decks/beta-blockers.js:81; scratch run: varix 8.1 mm at 'diuretics'

### pregnancy (`src/ui/decks/pregnancy.js`)

A coherent single-patient deck. The gestation slider, the compare buttons for propranolol and a scope whose red wale signs agree with the model (wall ratio 0.82) make it engaging. The issues: - The scope slide's rule ('without a scope in the year before conception') contradicts the case, who had an EGD last year. - The title makes propranolol the only beta-blocker. EASL 2023 allows beta-blockers including carvedilol and stresses banding for high-risk varices (extract; check the PDF). - The case has CSPH before conception, a Baveno VIII indication for a beta-blocker that the deck never mentions. - 'Her medicines are reviewed' is vague for autoimmune hepatitis: mycophenolate must stop before conception. Postpartum AIH flares are missing. - The postpartum slide can drop its 'model returns at once' caveat by using a 42-day ramp, which works in the engine.

Render check: Item 13 (pregnancy) is partly confirmed. As specified, keeping params { gestation: 0 }, the new lapse starts from the already postpartum state. The tiles go 5.4 to 5.4 L, 5.4 to 5.2 L/min and 10.8 to 11.0 mmHg, so nothing visibly falls, and mid-lapse the ramp pushes the figure back toward 37 weeks. The end numbers match the claim: 5359 mL ('5.4 L') and 11.0 mmHg. Dropping the params fixes it: the lapse then plays from delivery, 7.2 L, 8.1 L/min and 11.3 mmHg, down to 5.4, 5.2 and 11.0.

**Keep:** One patient through pregnancy, with the 'Weeks pregnant' whatIf on gestation. The model reproduces blood volume +32% and cardiac output +42% at 24 weeks.; propranolol slide: compare 'No beta-blocker' against 'Propranolol' moves the HVPG from 13.3 to 11.1 and the varix from 5.5 to 3.4 mm live.; scope slide: the red wale marks the line names appear in the model (wall ratio 0.82 > 0.7), so the words and the scope agree.; Checks on bleeding timing, endoscopy timing, fetal and neonatal monitoring, and avoiding vasopressin and terlipressin are accurate and US-appropriate.; The {bv} and {hvpg} tokens in the 'after' line land on the lapse's true end values

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `scope`
  - Now: line: 'Without a scope in the year before conception, screen in the second trimester. The varix now measures {varix}, with red wale marks.' / vignette (before): '... an EGD last year showed small varices.'
  - Problem: The case did have an EGD in the year before conception, so the rule in the line does not apply to her, yet the slide scopes her. The real reason is that her known small varices can grow quickly in pregnancy; the notes say so. The line also has two sentences.
  - Change: line as proposed. check 2 answer: 'In the second trimester, unless one was done in the year before conception; known varices are scoped again, because they can grow fast.'
  - Source: EASL CPG on liver diseases in pregnancy, J Hepatol 2023;79:768-828 ('upper endoscopy is suggested in the second trimester (if not performed within 1 year before conception)', per search extract); AASLD reproductive health guidance, Sarkar M et al., Hepatology 2021;73:318-365
- **Accuracy, medium, uncertain**, slide `propranolol`
  - Now: title: 'Propranolol is the beta-blocker in pregnancy' / line: 'It lowers the HVPG to {hvpg} and the varix to {varix}. Band ligation is the choice when it cannot be taken.'
  - Problem: Three problems: - The title makes propranolol the only option. A search extract of EASL 2023 says beta-blockers, carvedilol included, may be started or continued in pregnancy, and that high-risk varices should be banded. Neither statement could be confirmed in the full text. - Propranolol does have the most pregnancy data, and that claim is safe. - The line opens with 'It', with no named actor, and has two sentences.
  - Change: Adopt the title 'Propranolol protects the varices in pregnancy' and the one-sentence line. Leave the notes unchanged.
  - Why uncertain: The EASL 2023 extract on carvedilol cannot be verified (digest only, low confidence). The existing notes already say that propranolol has the longer record and carvedilol much less (pregnancy.js:64), so the proposed notes change is redundant. The current title is defensible: AASLD 2021 favors propranolol in pregnancy, from memory. The line fix (name the actor, one sentence) is good on its own.

## Treatment


### treatment (`src/ui/decks/treatment.js`)

Good framing (lower the inflow, lower the resistance, or go around it) and a strong banding slide, but it is the weakest of the four. (1) The 'vs' compare reads from a state after banding and 60 days of TIPS, so with delta 'target', propranolol and octreotide appear to raise the PPG. (2) The case (ascites, large varices, never bled) never gets a decision, and TIPS is shown to someone who has no indication for it. (3) The carvedilol note quotes Baveno VIII's statement for compensated CSPH rather than the one for ascites with varices. (4) The terlipressin/HRS slide is off-topic for a US deck on bleeding, its line opens with a fragment, and it implies kidney effects the model cannot show; the 'after' slide also credits the kidneys. The drug slides overlap beta-blockers, circuit and variceal-bleed, and merging them would free two slides. The cost slide should add rifaximin before TIPS and the EASL 2025 position that there is no absolute MELD cut-off.

**Keep:** The 'target' slide's framing: inflow, resistance or bypass, with a case that the rest of the deck follows.; The 'band' slide: banding closes the varix but not the pressure, with a caveat that keeps the model's 1.5 mmHg rise from being taken as the reason varices return.; The 'cost' slide: the origin lens plus the split readout show where the portal blood goes after TIPS.; The summary table with a 'How it works' note column, each row against the untreated patient.

- **Teaching, high, confirmed**, slide `vs`
  - Now: id: 'vs', cam: 'liver', data: 'ladder', key: ['ppg'], tiles: ['liver'], delta: 'target', compare: [TIPS (own), Propranolol, Carvedilol, Octreotide, Terlipressin] ... notes: 'In the model the drugs lower the PPG by 1 to 2 mmHg, carvedilol most...'
  - Problem: The buttons switch TIPS off in the state after 4 bands and 60 days on TIPS. In that state the collaterals have regressed (shunt 38% with TIPS off, against 75% at baseline) and the bands add about 1.6 mmHg. Taking the TIPS away therefore leaves a PPG of 20.8 mmHg, against 17.8 on 'target'. The data card counts from 'target', so these appear as rises: propranolol reads 18.7 (↑0.9), octreotide 19.2 (↑1.4), terlipressin 18.1 (↑0.3) and carvedilol 16.7 (↓1.1), against TIPS 9.1. The same drug gave 15.7 on the 'prop' slide. Measured against no treatment, the drugs lower the PPG by 1.6 to 4.1 mmHg, not 1 to 2. Terlipressin is not a US bleeding drug, and the deck's own summary leaves it out.
  - Change: As proposed, with notes 'about 1.5 to 3.5 mmHg from no treatment', and either change vs's kicker to 'Shunt' and remove the 'Compared' section, or move the whole 'After TIPS' pair before 'vs'.
  - Source: src/ui/presenter.js:1523-1535 (liveSet passes ref = refOf(s, i) to the data card), :2014-2015 (delta); my runs local run states.mjs of the chain with each option
- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `carv`
  - Now: Baveno VIII recommends a non-selective beta-blocker, preferably carvedilol, in compensated cirrhosis with clinically significant portal hypertension, to prevent decompensation. Watch the blood pressure, especially with ascites.
  - Problem: This case has ascites, so it is decompensated. The statement that applies is Baveno VIII's new one: with ascites and varices, carvedilol is preferred, to prevent a first bleed and ascites complications, with stated stop rules.
  - Change: As proposed. Add one clause for US practice if it fits: 'in the US, watch the blood pressure closely at the start'.
  - Source: Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026 (LoE 3, strong, new, 81%); CARVE-AS, Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121(6); Reiberger T et al., Gut 2013
- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `after`
  - Now: line: 'With sinusoidal pressure lower, less lymph forms and the kidneys excrete more sodium. The PPG stays at {ppg}.'
  - Problem: The model has no kidneys, so the second half of the first sentence describes something the figure cannot show, and there is no caveat. The notes say 'low platelets often persist', yet the model's platelets rise from 57 to 99 x 10³/µL over the 60 days. The slide could plot the mechanism it does model.
  - Change: As proposed, plus change the ask answer to 'The ascites clears as less fluid leaks from the sinusoids (in patients sodium excretion recovers too), and the low gradient keeps varices from rebleeding.'
  - Source: src/ui/flow-time.js:21-25 (sin and asc are allowed lines); deck-authoring-guide.md section 3.1 (caveat example); EASL CPG on TIPS 2025
- **Teaching, medium, confirmed**, slide `tips`
  - Now: vignette (target): 'A 58-year-old woman with MASH cirrhosis has ascites controlled on spironolactone and furosemide. A surveillance EGD shows large esophageal varices. She has never bled.' / tips notes: 'Indications: pre-emptive TIPS in high-risk variceal bleeding, rescue ..., recurrent bleeding, and recurrent or refractory ascites.'
  - Problem: The case never gets an answer, and the deck then gives her a TIPS and clears her ascites over 60 days. Her ascites is controlled and she has never bled, so she has no indication. Learners may take TIPS as first-line prophylaxis.

It constricts the dilated splanchnic arterioles, so blood pressure and kidney perfusion rise. ...', notes: 'It is avoided with hypoxia (SpO2 below 90%), volume overload or a creatinine of 5 mg/dL or more...', ask: ['What is given with terlipressin for HRS-AKI?', 'Albumin.']
  - Problem: The line opens with a sentence fragment, and it claims a rise in kidney perfusion that the model cannot show; there is no caveat. HRS is off-topic in a deck on lowering portal pressure, and the summary leaves terlipressin out. The FDA label wording is softer than 'avoided': do not start below an SpO2 of 90%; volume overload and ACLF grade 3 raise the risk; a creatinine of 5 mg/dL or more makes benefit unlikely. The ask is recall.

Line: 'Terlipressin, a vasopressin analogue, constricts the splanchnic arterioles more than octreotide, so portal inflow falls to {pvFlow} and the blood pressure rises to {map}.' Caveat: 'The model has no kidneys: in HRS-AKI, terlipressin with albumin works by raising the pressure that perfuses them.' Notes: 'In the US terlipressin is approved for HRS-AKI, not for variceal bleeding, and is given with albumin. Do not start it when the SpO2 is under 90%; volume overload and ACLF grade 3 raise the risk of respiratory failure, and a creatinine of 5 mg/dL or more makes benefit unlikely. Where it is unavailable, midodrine and octreotide with albumin, or norepinephrine in the ICU, are used.' Ask: ['Why does terlipressin raise the blood pressure while it lowers the portal pressure?', 'It constricts the splanchnic arterioles: less blood enters the portal vein, and the systemic resistance rises.'].

Ask answer: 'Severe heart failure or pulmonary hypertension, uncontrolled infection, refractory encephalopathy; a high MELD raises the risk.' Update checks[2] to 'a third to a half'.

### beta-blockers (`src/ui/decks/beta-blockers.js`)

A well-built advanced deck: two drugs in two kinds of resistance, live compare buttons, honest model caveats, and a check that catches the misleading HVPG. Three problems are about evidence. (1) The size of carvedilol's extra HVPG fall: the deck says 'about 2 mmHg' but cites a 2025 seven-trial meta-analysis that found 0.8 mmHg. (2) The '4 mmHg more' blood pressure figure, which I could not confirm. (3) The attribution to Baveno VIII and EASL 2025 on carvedilol in PSVD, which no digest could confirm. The choice slide should name Baveno VIII's new statement for ascites with varices and give the doses. Two unused display features would show the deck's thesis directly: the table's liver-resistance column (carvedilol lowers R, propranolol does not) and the ladder's misleads and works brackets. 7 of 8 lines run 2 to 3 sentences.

**Keep:** The 2x2 structure (propranolol and carvedilol, sinusoidal and presinusoidal) with its 'delta' and 'vs' choices, so each row is compared with the right reference.; The compare buttons on carv-sin and carv-pre: one patient, two drugs.; Quick check 3 (the HVPG falls on carvedilol in schistosomiasis, but did the portal pressure?), which tests the deck's hardest idea.; Caveats that say plainly where the model differs from trials (the blood pressure barely moves).

- **Accuracy, medium, confirmed**, slide `carv-sin`
  - Now: In trials carvedilol lowers the HVPG by about 2 mmHg more than propranolol. (summary notes: 'The trial figures come from a 2025 meta-analysis of seven trials in cirrhosis (Joshi)...')
  - Problem: The 2025 meta-analysis of seven RCTs (351 patients, J Gastroenterol Hepatol) found a mean extra fall of 0.76 mmHg (95% CI 0.08 to 1.45). 2.2 mmHg comes from the older 2014 meta-analysis of 4 trials. If 'Joshi' is the 2025 seven-trial paper, the slide contradicts its own source. In the model the extra fall is 1.4 mmHg (10.3 to 8.9).
  - Change: As proposed. Note that treatment/vs and one-year/carv say 'about a fifth', which is the total HVPG fall, not the extra fall over propranolol, so they are not inconsistent with this.
  - Source: Meta-analysis of 7 RCTs, 351 patients, J Gastroenterol Hepatol 2025 (MD −0.76 mmHg, 95% CI −1.45 to −0.08; author not confirmed); Sinagra E et al., Ann Hepatol 2014;13(4); model run: ohm 11.6, prop-sin 10.3, carv-sin 8.9 mmHg

  - Change: Apply it to 'schisto' only: data 'ladder', key ['pv'], brackets { hvpg: 'misleads', ppg: 'works' }, no hvpg tile. Leave carv-pre on tiles.

### shunts (`src/ui/decks/shunts.js`)

The best physics deck in this group. The parallel-resistance equation, the stent-width slider and the BRTO buttons teach the shunt trade well. The main gap is current guidance on encephalopathy. ACG 2026 and Baveno VIII put spontaneous-shunt embolization and TIPS narrowing at the center of refractory HE, and the engine can close C6 live (PPG 10.1 to 18.8 mmHg, liver flow 34 to 63%), but no slide does it. Three of the lines run three sentences and 40 words, and three slides carry 6 to 8 sentences of notes. The Warren slide claims decompression the model does not show: the varix stays 'Large' at 5.9 mm, because the engine cuts only the splenic vein and keeps the left gastric vein. BRTO and closing a shunt grow esophageal varices at once. The summary's 'Shunted' column (splenorenal 100% against TIPS 93%) clashes with its typed encephalopathy words. Swapping it for the varix column shows both the benefit and the cost.

**Keep:** The 'own' slide's equation 1/R_total = 1/R_liver + 1/R_shunt, with the inflow explanation in the ask.; The whatIf on tips.d (6-12 mm) with the TIPS Doppler and delta 'own'.; The BRTO compare (shunt open / closed) with delta 'grs'.; The summary's single trade-off sentence: lower gradient against less portal blood for the liver.

  - Problem: Embolizing a large spontaneous shunt is a current guideline option for refractory HE (ACG 2026, conditional; Baveno VIII after TIPS), and the engine can do it live. The slide only mentions it. Engine result on closing C6: PPG 10.1 to 18.8 mmHg, liver flow 34 to 63%, shunted 100 to 61%, portal flow from reversed (-0.23) to hepatopetal (0.64 L/min), varix 2.1 to 8.3 mm.
  - Change: Replace the srs notes sentence 'Large shunts go with recurrent or persistent encephalopathy, and closing one by embolization can control it in selected patients.' with the review's two sentences merged into one, so the notes stay at 4.

  - Now: (no deck on hepatic encephalopathy; HE appears in shunts/srs, wide, treatment/cost)
  - Problem: Encephalopathy is the third decompensating event and has no deck, although ACG 2026 published the first US HE guideline in over a decade. The model can show its portal-flow side (shunt fraction, liver flow, origin lens, shunt closure, stent width), not ammonia or the brain.
  - Change: New deck 'encephalopathy' (advanced, 10 min, 8 slides), if the owner wants it; otherwise fold slides 3 and 5 into shunts. 1 'firstpass': preset healthy, lens 'origin', tiles ['shunt','liver'] (all gut blood passes the liver). 2 'collat': preset cirr-decomp, lens origin, vignette of covert HE (failed driving test). 3 'srs': spontaneous C6, days 30, Doppler PV_TRUNK, vignette of recurrent HE on lactulose and rifaximin. 4 'tips': tips on, whatIf tips.d 6-12. 5 'emb': compare occluded C6 open/closed. 6 'price': occluded C6, days 60, lapse { seconds: 7 }, tiles ['asc','varix','ppg'] (model: ascites 1.24 L, varix 7.8 mm after 60 days). 7 'narrow': tips d 12 to 6 compare. 8 summary table cols ['shunt','liver','ppg','varix']. Each slide needs the caveat 'The model has no ammonia or brain; the share of portal blood that skips the liver stands in for the risk.' Notes: lactulose to 2 or 3 soft stools, rifaximin added for recurrence, no routine ammonia (ACG 2026). The work overlaps shunts/srs and wide, so split the material between the two decks.
  - Why uncertain: The numbers check out: occluded C6 plus 60 days gives ascites 1.24 L, varix 7.8 mm, PPG 18.0. Whether to build it is the owner's decision. The model has no ammonia or brain, and its HE index is only the shunt fraction scaled by cirrhosis (metrics.js:69), so every slide would need the caveat. It would also overlap shunts/srs and wide. Folding the embolization (shunts-1) and narrowing slides into shunts gets most of the value without a new deck.
  - Source: ACG Clinical Guideline: Hepatic Encephalopathy, Am J Gastroenterol 2026;121(3):588-618; EASL CPG HE, J Hepatol 2022;77:807-824; scratch run of occluded C6 + 60 days

### tips-doppler (`src/ui/decks/tips-doppler.js`)

This is the most interactive deck in this area: three-point gate buttons, a movable narrowing, a stenosis slider on the left portal vein, ghost readings and honest caveats. Its clinical content matches Mitchell 2025 and the bare-stent cut-offs. It has four problems: - It runs to 13 slides, over the 12-slide cap. - The 'shunted' slide is mainly TIPS physiology that the shunts and two-inflows decks already teach. It also states the model's 'three quarters' bypass figure as clinical fact. - It never states the target gradient (PPG under 12 mmHg) or the EASL 2025 rules for when to suspect dysfunction. It also never says that a normal Doppler does not rule out dysfunction once symptoms return. - Objective 4 is about reading the app's colors, not a clinical skill. Most lines run 2 sentences, several notes run 5-6, and the notes carry UI instructions ('Tap...', 'Drag...') that print in the handout.

**Keep:** The three-point gate on 'jet', 'narrow' and 'where' (compare with gate and stenosisSite). The 'where' ask (60, 240 and 65 cm/s: where is the narrowing?) is an application question.; 'warn': the stenosis slider on the left portal vein Doppler shows a late, insensitive sign turning back as the stent narrows.; The caveats, which say where the model differs (bare-stent cut-offs, a short pinch, segment differences), and the Mitchell 2025 velocities in the notes.; The Doppler ghosts on 'mpv' and 'backup' (delta 'before' and 'mpv') and the 'fix' compare (Narrowed, Dilated): a full surveillance story on one patient.

- **Out of date, medium, confirmed**, slide `fix`
  - Now: A Doppler that suggests stenosis is followed by TIPS venography, with the portosystemic gradient measured across the stent.
  - Problem: Under EASL 2025, dysfunction is suspected clinically: bleeding or ascites returns, new ascites appears, or ascites has not cleared 4-8 weeks after the TIPS. The work-up runs stepwise: Doppler, then angiography, then a pressure measurement. With covered stents, Doppler misses some dysfunction (routine Doppler surveillance after PTFE TIPS rarely found a problem before symptoms did). A normal scan should not stop venography when the problem has returned. The deck teaches only the Doppler-first route.
  - Change: First notes sentence: 'Suspect dysfunction when bleeding or ascites returns, or when ascites is slow to clear in the months after the TIPS.' Keep '4 to 8 weeks' only if the EASL 2025 PDF confirms it. Use the rest as proposed.
  - Source: EASL CPG on TIPS, J Hepatol 2025 (dysfunction statement, strong; Doppler, angiography, pressure measurement in that order; from search extracts of the Guideline Central summary); Amsterdam UMC cohort 'Doppler follow-up after TIPS placement is not routinely indicated' (https://pure.amsterdamumc.nl/en/publications/doppler-follow-up-after-tips-placement-is-not-routinely-indicated/)
- **Cut, medium, confirmed**, slide `shunted`
  - Now: In decompensated cirrhosis, collaterals already carry about three quarters of the gut and spleen blood past the liver, mostly through the gastroesophageal varices.
  - Problem: The deck has 13 slides; rule 12 caps decks at 12. 'shunted' teaches the shunt fraction and encephalopathy risk, not Doppler, and shunts/tips (tiles shunt and liver) and two-inflows/tips already cover them. 'Three quarters, mostly through the gastroesophageal varices' is the model's own figure, stated as clinical fact. Measured shunt fractions vary widely in patients.
  - Change: Delete 'shunted' and its tests/deck-pills.json entry. Add the proposed sentence to the 'stent' notes.
  - Source: deck-authoring-guide.md section 9 rule 12; shunts.js:48-52; two-inflows.js:70-74; scratch run (shunt 0.8 before, 0.9 with the stent)
- **Addition, medium, confirmed**, slide `stent`
  - Now: A covered stent joins the [right portal vein](RPV) to the right hepatic vein. Colored by flow, it is the busiest vessel in the liver, and the PPG falls from {ppg@before} before the TIPS to {ppg}.
  - Problem: The deck never states the hemodynamic goal of a TIPS. EASL 2025 recommends the smallest stent diameter that brings the portosystemic gradient under 12 mmHg, the same number as the app's red PPG cut-off. The model reaches 10.6 mmHg here. The notes' 'here 9 mm wide' also needs context: covered stents are 8 or 10 mm, and controlled-expansion grafts are set between them.
  - Change: line: 'A covered stent joins the [right portal vein](RPV) to the right hepatic vein, and colored by flow it is the busiest vessel in the liver: the PPG falls from {ppg@before} to {ppg}, under the {<12 mmHg} target.' notes, first sentence: 'A covered stent, here dilated to 9 mm (controlled-expansion grafts are set between 8 and 10 mm), runs from the right portal vein to the right hepatic vein; the aim is the smallest width that brings the PPG under 12 mmHg.'
  - Source: EASL CPG on TIPS, J Hepatol 2025;83:177-210 (smallest diameter for a gradient under 12 mmHg, LoE 2, strong, per literature digest); shunts.js:49 (same target); scratch run (ppg 10.6 on 'stent')
- **Teaching, medium, confirmed**, slide `deck`
  - Now: objectives[3]: 'Read the figure colored by velocity, flow, direction, congestion and blood origin'; summary: '... with the vessels colored by velocity, flow, direction, congestion and blood origin.'
  - Problem: The objective is about the app, not a clinical skill. The five-item color list is the humanizer's 'list for rhythm' pattern. The deck's real fourth outcome, planning surveillance and knowing when to go to venography, is unstated.
  - Change: objectives[3]: 'Plan TIPS follow-up: the baseline scan, what each later scan is compared with, and when to go to venography'. summary: 'Doppler of a TIPS: the scan before the stent, a working stent, a narrowed one, and what to do when it narrows.'
  - Source: docs/ai-humanizer.md (rule-of-three lists, say the fact); deck-authoring-guide.md section 2 (objectives start with a verb)
- **Humanizer, medium, confirmed**, slide `before, jet, mpv, narrow, where, summary`
  - Now: narrow: 'Six months later, fluid reappears around the liver on ultrasound. At the narrowing, in the distal stent, a jet runs over {>190 cm/s}, the proximal and mid stent have slowed under {<90 cm/s}, and the PPG is back to {ppg}.'
  - Problem: 12 of 13 lines run two sentences (rule 1). narrow, where and summary run 38-40 words. 'before' types the cut-off but not the patient's live velocity, which is a tile key on that slide.
  - Change: Minor: in 'mpv' keep the cut-off as a pill ('a later fall under {<30 cm/s}'), as in the current line. In 'before', end on 'and these readings are the baseline for every later scan' rather than a colon reveal.
  - Source: deck-authoring-guide.md section 9 rules 1 and 6; docs/ai-humanizer.md; scratch run (pvVel 14.2 cm/s on 'before')
- **Humanizer, low, confirmed**, slide `jet, narrow, where, warn, fix, shunted`
  - Now: jet notes: '... Tap Proximal, Mid or Distal to move the Doppler gate; that third of the stent glows green.' narrow: 'The tiles compare with the working stent. Tap Proximal, Mid or Distal to move the gate along the stent.'
  - Problem: Notes are printed in the learner handout (handout.js:98-113), where there are no buttons. UI instructions to the presenter break 'student-facing only'. jet and narrow run to 6 sentences and where to 5 (rule 11).
  - Change: As proposed, with the jet-notes fix to the 'narrow far less often' logic (see doppler-12).
  - Source: docs/ai-humanizer.md (teaching-card rules: student-facing only); deck-authoring-guide.md section 9 rule 11; src/ui/handout.js:98-113

## New decks proposed inside the review groups

The ranked list in DECK_REVIEW.md supersedes these. They are kept for their detail.


### new: foundations predict-and-reveal

Idea for a new foundation deck of retrieval practice. It uses the question-then-answer pairs of Find the resistance, applied to the foundation mechanisms, with the live buttons as the reveal. It reuses foundation content on purpose, as retrieval practice, and it is the closest the presenter gets to the blueprint's Predict, Observe, Explain step.

- **Addition, low, uncertain**, slide `new`: The foundations have no predict step. The blueprint's teaching principle P4 asks learners to predict before the model runs, and the presenter's nearest tool is the q/a slide pair used only in Find the resistance.
  - Change: a4: tool { kind: 'doppler', vessel: 'PVH_R', delta: 'q4' } with lens 'direction'. Confirm with the owner before building.

### proposed new deck: csph-nit ('CSPH without a catheter')

This is a case-practice companion to the stiffness deck, built like where-resistance: a question slide, then an answer slide. It teaches the Baveno VIII and AASLD decisions that the stiffness deck only states: rule out, gray zone, rule in, who gets carvedilol, who gets a scope, and the three pitfalls. Every number below comes from the engine. Level advanced, about 15 minutes, 11 slides, kickers 'Case 1' to 'Case 5' plus Summary.

- **Addition, medium, uncertain**, slide `new`: The stiffness rules are taught as statements in stiffness/rule, spleen/screen, endoscopy and varices, but never applied to cases. Baveno VIII's gray-zone logic (ANTICIPATE at 75% or more, a spleen stiffness over 55 kPa at 100 Hz, a repeat at 12 months) and the pitfalls are exactly what residents get wrong.
  - Change: If built: end on a table with cols ['lsm','plt','spleen','hvpg'] (put a scale slide before it if wanted). Give case 2 a different person and cause (for example, a woman with hepatitis C or non-obese MASLD). Add sections. Case 3's answer compare is acute and lands near LSM 12.5 kPa; check it on the preview.

### new deck: refractory-bleed

Proposed companion to variceal-bleed, 8 slides, advanced, about 8 minutes. It covers bleeding that drugs and banding do not control: the balloon-tube bridge, rescue TIPS and its futility limits, and a bleeding fundal varix, with the choice between BRTO and TIPS. It uses engine features that no deck uses yet: balloonEso, balloonGas, a gastric varix rupture (site 'GV') and stopBleed. I ran each step through the slide chain. One engine fact shapes the design. The chain never clots an open bleed, because clotting runs only on the live clock (engine.js:570). A rescue TIPS alone therefore leaves the chain bleeding at a low rate, so the deck stops the bleed explicitly under the balloon. This matches what the live clock would do after 45 seconds (engine.js:664-668).

- **Addition, medium, confirmed**, slide `new`: Refractory bleeding, balloon tamponade, rescue TIPS and fundal variceal bleeding appear in no deck except in passing. variceal-bleed cannot add them, because after 4 bands a second tear loses only about 13 mL in the model.
  - Change: Start with a calm slide (preset and vignette, no action) and tear on slide 2. Reword 'fail': 'Bleeding continues despite attempted banding, and the heart rate climbs to {hr}.' Summary: give gvballoon and choose vs: 'gv'. Use causesHead 'Likely futile, unless a transplant is near' with two causes. Caveat on 'balloon': 'In patients the gastric balloon goes up first; the model shows only the esophageal one.' Do not name Baveno VII in learner text: futility is a Baveno VII statement not confirmed in VIII. Re-check all numbers on the preview.
- **Engine opportunity, low, confirmed**, slide `rescue`: In the slide chain, an open bleed keeps flowing through every settle while P minus the external pressure is above 0 (engine.js:456), and it never clots. I ran a rescue TIPS without stopBleed: the chain lost another 550 to 850 mL, and the MAP fell to 69 mmHg on the following slide. Live, the same state would clot after 45 seconds.
  - Change: Keep the explicit stopBleed. Do not run the clot rule during settles unless variceal-bleed and endoscopy are re-tuned and their thresholds re-tested.

## Across decks, by review group

Points each group raised about more than one deck, and what the verifiers found that the reviewers missed.


### foundations

- The SOS HVPG differs by deck. Preset sos (scenario.js:91, post 20) gives 8.9 mmHg in Sites, Find the resistance and Lobule. Outflow overrides it to post 32 and shows 11.3 mmHg. Lobule and Outflow both say 'above 10 mmHg supports the diagnosis', and Sites says 'about 9, under 10'. EBMT 2023 uses 10 mmHg or more. Either raise the preset's post value (about 30) or set Outflow's params wherever SOS is the teaching point, and reword to '10 mmHg or more'.
- Lobule anatomy is taught three to four times: circulation (lobule, triad, wall, central), lobule (lobule, zones), two-inflows/meet, and ascites (start, walls, lymph). The circulation/lobule and lobule/lobule notes are nearly verbatim ('The classic lobule is a hexagon about a millimeter across...'), and circulation/triad repeats two-inflows/meet.
- The same tour of resistance sites runs in seven decks: sites, lobule, where-resistance, hvpg, ncph, outflow and beta-blockers. Schistosomiasis is a preset slide in eight decks, and 'the wedge reads the sinusoids' is a slide in hvpg, ncph, lobule and where-resistance.
- The healthy meal time-lapse appears twice (circulation/flow, circuit/meal), with the same caveat 'about a quarter'. The model shows +29% (1.09 to 1.41 L/min); MRI shows +137% in healthy volunteers (Cox, Eur Radiol Exp 2018). Use one wording in both decks.
- Duplicate title: circuit/tips and treatment/tips are both 'TIPS lowers the gradient by bypassing the liver'.
- Near-identical case cards for preset cirr-decomp: sites/sin, shunts/own, two-inflows/cirr and lobule/cirr all use 'ascites despite spironolactone and furosemide' and 'platelets 57,000/µL'. Hepatofugal flow: two-inflows/fugal and doppler/reverse (a 66- vs 67-year-old woman with MASH, encephalopathy admissions, albumin 2.6 g/dL). A JAK2-positive myeloproliferative neoplasm appears in both sites/pvt and two-inflows/clot. The 'despite diuretics' wording also clashes with the model's grade 1 ascites (1.06 L).
- Hepatofugal flow is taught in both two-inflows/fugal and doppler/reverse, with the same preset and the same 'about 8%'. Give each a distinct angle: Two inflows takes the oxygen and arterioportal angle, Doppler the waveform.
- The carvedilol mechanism is stated as fact in circuit/drugs and treatment/carv ('relaxes the stellate cells') but hedged in beta-blockers ('most likely', 'is thought to'). Use the hedged wording everywhere.
- The hyperdynamic circulation (blueprint goal 1, curriculum lesson 4) has no slide in any deck; it appears once, in the circuit/both notes. The co tile is used only in Pregnancy, though the engine shows cardiac output rising from 5.0 to 6.7 L/min and gut arteriole resistance falling about 40% from healthy to decompensated.
- The 'liver' tile (flow through the sinusoids, % of normal) is called 'liver blood flow' or 'total liver blood flow' in two-inflows, and 'liver perfusion' in treatment. After a TIPS the tile reads 31% while hepatic artery inflow is 0.88 L/min. Use one learner word, 'flow through the liver', in every deck.
- The hepatic artery is a hidden edge (anatomy.js:15-20). Any deck line that describes the artery on the figure (two-inflows/switch, two-inflows/dual) cannot be seen; check other decks that use lens 'origin' or 'flow' on cam 'liver' for the same claim.
- Statins are mentioned only in lobule/cirr, as a drug that lowers intrahepatic tone. Baveno VIII downgraded them, so either qualify the mention or remove it, to keep the app on one message.
- Consistent across decks, keep as is: portal vein about three quarters of liver blood flow (circulation, two-inflows, checks); about three quarters of the raised intrahepatic resistance fixed and a quarter dynamic (circuit/resist, lobule/cirr); HVPG normal 1 to 5 mmHg and CSPH at 10 mmHg or more; PPG normal 5 mmHg or less.

Found by the verifier:

- circuit/both (src/ui/decks/circuit.js:68): the cirrhotic 'meal' runs 8 disease days. In the model, ascites rises from 3 mL to 844 mL (Grade 1; 1 day already gives 540 mL), and the varix grows from 3.9 to 5.6 mm ('Large'). A meal should change neither. An acute splanchnicTone 0.72 patch gives the same PPG rise (13.6 to 15.7), so the lesson does not need the days. Check whether the circuit figure shows the fluid or the varix. Consider a caveat, or have the next slide reset the patient (parallel already reloads cirr-decomp).
- two-inflows/switch (two-inflows.js:46): in the engine the buffer eases with a time constant of about 10 s (engine.js:621-623). In a direct simulation, turning it off took liver flow from 72% to about 59% at 7 s, 39-46% at 20-30 s and 31% at 60 s (noisy, beat to beat). liveSet refreshes the data card for 7 s (presenter.js:1529-1533), so 'Buffer off' may show about 55-60% while the line says 'about 40%'. Check on the preview; if confirmed, word the line as 'falls toward 40%' or lengthen the refresh.
- circulation/summary line (circulation.js:92): 'with an HVPG of 1 to 5 and a PPG of 5 or less' gives numbers without units (guide rule 13, :501).
- SOS cut-off wording is inconsistent across decks: 'above 10 mmHg' in lobule.js:55, outflow.js:69-70 and sites.js:66. The review flags lobule and outflow but not sites.js:66; any change to '10 mmHg or more' should cover all three.
- Several review citations are off: docs/ai-humanizer.md:115 is the drug before/after example, not 'full sentences' (that is :122 and guide rule 13 at :501); COLS is table-marks.js:102, not :99; the liver tile is table-marks.js:55. Counts are slightly off: circulation has 9 of 11 multi-sentence lines (not 8) and 4 notes over four sentences (not 5).
- Clinical citations I could not verify because the web search budget was exhausted and WebFetch failed DNS: Cox EF et al., Eur Radiol Exp 2018;2:26 (portal flow +137%/+67%, hepatic artery -30% after a meal), PMC3592829 (postprandial HVPG +26%), and the exact EBMT 2023 HVPG wording ('≥10' against '>10'). Treat those numbers as unconfirmed before they go into notes or caveats.

### sites-causes

- Budd–Chiari HVPG: three decks give three answers. sites/post and sites/summary show a measured HVPG of 0.3 mmHg. hvpg/summary blanks it ('Cannot be measured', hvpg.js:88). outflow/summary calls it 'meaningless' (outflow.js:85-86). Fix: blank the BCS row in sites/summary and keep the 'where a catheter can reach behind them' hedge on sites/post.
- SOS cut-off wording: sites/postsin, outflow/soswedge (line and notes), lobule/sos and hvpg/summary notes all say 'above 10 mmHg'. EBMT 2023 (Bone Marrow Transplant 2023, PMID 37095231) says an HVPG of 10 mmHg or more. Use '10 mmHg or more' everywhere; tests/deck-thresholds.test.js KNOWN['outflow/soswedge'] checks hvpg > 10 and would stay valid.
- The schisto preset's sinusoidal pressure is 9.5 mmHg, rated amber 'High' (table-marks.js:12). ncph/tract ('the sinusoids beyond stay at {sin}') and ascites/presin ('leaves sinusoidal pressure near normal', sin tile as key) both say the sinusoids are near normal next to a 'High' tile. Change the words to 'only mildly raised' rather than the preset, which feeds the numbers of many decks.
- Duplicate patients across decks (guide section 14, 'Vary the people'): where/q1 = hvpg intro case (57-year-old man, 88,000/µL, 24 kPa); where/q3 ≈ right-heart/constrict (man about 60, Hodgkin radiation); where/q6 = right-heart/web (36-year-old man from Nepal); where/q5 and ncph/patient are both from Lake Victoria; the Budd–Chiari template 'has had RUQ pain for 2 months and now has tense ascites' appears in sites/post, outflow/bc and doppler/bc.
- Carvedilol in presinusoidal disease: ncph/nsbb credits the claim to 'EASL 2025 and Baveno VIII' (unverified). beta-blockers/summary notes cite 'Razafindrazoto 2022, de Abreu 2022'. I verified Razafindrazoto (JGH Open 2022, n = 61) but could not find a de Abreu 2022 study. Have both decks cite the verified trial and drop the guideline attribution.
- Splenic vein thrombosis is taught twice with the same lens ('origin') and the same pancreatitis plus fundal varices case: varices/svt and prehepatic/svt. Make them complement each other: varices keeps 'origin' (where the blood goes); prehepatic takes the unused 'delta' lens plus a splenicRx compare (the cure).
- The 'soft liver with varices means resistance before the sinusoids' sentence appears almost word for word in ncph/stiff, prehepatic/liver and stiffness/schisto. That is fine across standalone decks, but vary the wording, and let ncph/stiff carry the PSVD-specific point (no NIT rule-out, scope at diagnosis).
- Wedge wording: sites/sin and where/a1 answer 'the resistance lies in the sinusoids the wedge reads', while sites/ladders, hvpg/wedge and lobule/summary say the resistance lies between the wedge and the free reading. Use one phrasing app-wide: the wedge reads the inlet of the sinusoids, upstream of their resistance.
- The 'HVPG misses presinusoidal resistance' idea is taught in nine decks (sites, where, ncph, prehepatic, hvpg, lobule, stiffness, ascites, beta-blockers), and the numbers agree because all use the same presets. The mixed-level case (presinusoidal plus sinusoidal) appears only as a verbal ask in lobule/schisto. The ncph 'mixed' slide and the where-2 deck would make it something the room can drag and read.

Found by the verifier:

- sites/sin notes (src/ui/decks/sites.js:57): 'In MASLD cirrhosis the wedge can read low, so an HVPG under 10 does not rule out CSPH' contradicts itself, because CSPH is defined as HVPG ≥10 (guide 8.6). Replace with 'In MASLD cirrhosis the wedge can read lower than the portal pressure, and some patients decompensate below 10 mmHg.' Source: Ferrusquia-Acosta, J Hepatol 2021;74:811-818.
- sites-6's proposed line is 47 words by the test's whitespace split, not 41, so it would fail tests/deck-line-length.test.js:10. Use the 41-word version given in the verdict.
- Duplicate patients the review did not list (guide section 14, 'Vary the people'): (1) The severe-TR heart-failure case appears five times: sites/cardiac (sites.js:84, 67-year-old man), doppler.js:96 (69-year-old man), varices.js:81 (73-year-old man), lobule.js:61 (71-year-old woman), stiffness.js:72 (70-year-old woman), mostly 'raised JVP, leg edema and ascites'. (2) sites/sin (sites.js:54) and lobule.js:45 share 'ascites despite spironolactone and furosemide, albumin 2.8 g/dL, platelets 57,000/µL'. (3) where/q5 and stiffness/schisto (stiffness.js:64) are both 33-year-old men with schistosomiasis. (4) The reviewer's own replacements clash: a 63-year-old woman (stiffness.js:39) and a 66-year-old woman (doppler.js:60).
- If ncph-4, ncph-6 and ncph-11 are all adopted, the 9-minute ncph deck carries three or four patients. nsbb and band would then treat the PSVD patient while the nsbb notes (ncph.js:64) speak of schistosomiasis, and ncph-9's change collides with ncph-3 on the same 45-word line. Decide them together, update minutes, and add new rows to ncph/summary.
- prehepatic-3 and spleen: splenectomy as a live compare option may not ease. metrics.js:111 sets platelets to 380 and spleen length to 0 at once, and stage.js:4394 sets the spleen's opacity to 0.14 directly. Check on the preview before relying on it; the owner's rule is that everything eases.
- Heart slides deliberately carry no works/misleads brackets anywhere (hvpg.js:72, right-heart.js, where.js:62, sites.js:80), so sites-3 (cardiac) and where-5 would change an app-wide teaching choice. Decide it once for all decks.
- Verification limit for this pass: the web search budget was exhausted, and WebFetch failed DNS (pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov). Several items rest on the digests or memory and need the owner's check: EBMT 2023 ≥10 mmHg for proven SOS, Plessier 2010's end-of-follow-up against 1-year recanalization rates, Heider 2004's 4% bleed rate, VALDIG 'incomplete septal fibrosis/cirrhosis', Razafindrazoto 2022, and the EASL 2025 vascular CPG doi/PMID and its 'suspect PSVD' statement. The ncph/nsbb guideline attribution was set on purpose in commit 1af29676, so it should not be removed until someone has read the PDFs.

### vascular-heart-pregnancy

- SOS threshold wording: EBMT 2023 counts an HVPG of 10 mmHg or more as proof. Five places say 'above 10 mmHg': - outflow.js:69 and :70 - lobule.js:55 - sites.js:66 - hvpg.js:91 endoscopy.js:38 also says 'above 10 mmHg (CSPH)'; CSPH is 10 mmHg or more. Change all to '10 mmHg or more', using the {>=10 mmHg} pill in lines.
- SOS HVPG differs between decks. sites/postsin and lobule/sos load preset 'sos' alone (HVPG about 9 mmHg; sites notes 'the model shows an earlier stage') while saying 10 mmHg or more supports the diagnosis. outflow/sos adds fibrosis post 32 and shows 11.3 mmHg. Either give lobule/sos the same params as outflow/sos, or keep the sites caveat in lobule as well.
- Repeated case-card people (guide section 14, 'Vary the people'): - right-heart/web is the same 36-year-old Nepali man as where-resistance/q6. - right-heart/constrict is the same Hodgkin mediastinal-radiation survivor as where-resistance/q3 (58 against 61 years). - right-heart/tr and doppler/rhf share the phrase 'liver pulses under the examining hand'. At least eight decks open with an older adult with severe TR, ascites and a raised JVP: right-heart/tr, doppler/rhf, sites/cardiac, stiffness/heart, varices/nograd, lobule/cong, ascites/hf, tap/heart. - outflow/bcs, doppler/bc, sites/post and ascites/bc all read '...has had RUQ pain for 2 months and now has tense ascites'.
- Repeated slides on the same idea: - The pulsatile portal vein in heart failure: right-heart/pulse and doppler/rhf. - 'HVPG normal, varices rare because every station is high': right-heart/cath, hvpg/heart, sites/cardiac, varices/nograd, lobule/cong, where a3. - 'The catheter cannot wedge in Budd–Chiari; Doppler is the first test': outflow/nowedge, hvpg/bc, doppler/bc, sites/post. Make right-heart and outflow the full treatments, and have the others keep a one-line pointer rather than a full re-teach.
- Terminology: right-heart/pulse says 'pulsatility index' and doppler/rhf says 'pulsatility fraction' with VExUS cut-offs of 30% and 50%. Use 'pulsatility fraction' in both.
- Typed numbers in asks drift silently, because asks are printed raw without token filling (handout.js:98-110). hvpg/heart asks 'WHVP 21, FHVP 20, right atrium 19 mmHg', while the model's whole-number ladder shows 20, 19 and 19. right-heart/cath asks 'Portal vein 21 mmHg and HVPG 1 mmHg', which matches today (20.5 and 0.6). Either word these asks without numbers, or add a test that checks the numbers against deck states.
- Management conflict: the pvt-cirrhosis case has CSPH but is banded four times before anticoagulation and never given a beta-blocker. The beta-blockers, one-year and stiffness decks teach the Baveno VIII rule that CSPH alone calls for carvedilol and makes screening endoscopy unnecessary. Align pvt-cirrhosis (see pvt-cirrhosis-1).
- Beta-blocker in pregnancy against elsewhere: the pregnancy deck's 'Propranolol is the beta-blocker in pregnancy' and the beta-blockers deck's 'carvedilol preferred' do not contradict each other. Still, beta-blockers could add one line ('In pregnancy, propranolol, with the longest record, is the usual choice') so the two decks point to each other. The propranolol titration target (55 to 60 a minute) is consistent across beta-blockers, treatment and pregnancy.
- IVC web placement: membranous IVC obstruction is a form of Budd–Chiari syndrome (right-heart notes and where a6), but the outflow deck, the Budd–Chiari deck, never mentions it. Add a one-sentence cross-reference to outflow/bcs notes ('A web in the IVC above the hepatic veins does the same; the right heart talk shows it'), or move the web slide there and keep right-heart purely cardiac.

Found by the verifier:

- pvt-cirrhosis/course and summary (pvt-cirrhosis.js:71, 77-80). If pvt-cirrhosis-1 is adopted, carvedilol drops the HVPG from 11.6 to 8.6 (engine run). The talk-trace title 'Portal pressure followed the clot while HVPG stayed flat', the summary's calm: ['hvpg'] and the 'band' row ('After banding') all become wrong. The review did not flag these knock-on edits.

Putting carvedilol on 'base' would drop the HVPG under 10 and clash with 'Cirrhosis with CSPH', so at least say in the base notes that CSPH found now calls for carvedilol (Baveno VIII, J Hepatol 2026, doi 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.07.030).
- pvt-cirrhosis/scope notes (pvt-cirrhosis.js:51). 'Baveno VIII advises screening for varices ... before anticoagulation starts' is an unverified attribution. The Baveno VIII vascular statement found by the digests says only that anticoagulation must not be delayed for endoscopic prophylaxis.
