The engine models constriction as a constant extra RA pressure (engine.js:303: raExt = pplDev + 12 * pericardial), so the on-screen Doppler cannot show that pattern. The slide needs a caveat.
- right-heart lines and notes over the house limits, not addressed. Lines with two sentences: normal (:22), cath (:44), constrict (:52, partly addressed) and summary (:68). Notes over 4 sentences (rule 11): normal 5, constrict 6, web 6 (5 after the reviewer's deletion).
- pvt-cirrhosis/course (pvt-cirrhosis.js:72-73). The line has two sentences, and the notes repeat it nearly word for word ('Recanalization is followed by imaging ... about every three months, not by the HVPG'). This is the humanizer's 'summary sentence repeating the one before'.
- pvt-cirrhosis-2 and pvt-cirrhosis-5 conflict as written. The notes rewrite in -2 deletes 'whether a partial clot worsens the outlook is uncertain', which -5's caveat cites as the deck's own position. It also deletes 'more often when treatment starts early', which the anticoag ask answer ('Starting it early') depends on (pvt-cirrhosis.js:67).
- pregnancy/scope notes against check 2 (pregnancy.js:54 and :18). The notes say a scope clear before pregnancy 'does not settle the question'; the check says screening is skipped if a scope was done in the year before conception. The two contradict each other, independent of the line fix.
- right-heart-6 and right-heart-14 conflict. The proposed TIPS slide's ask types 'a portal vein of 20 mmHg', the drift the reviewer flags in right-heart-14. Its notes also say the shunt 'carries little', while the engine shunts 29% of portal flow and cuts liver flow from 79% to 72%.
- Verification limits. The web search budget for this turn was exhausted on the first query, and WebFetch failed DNS (pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov). The following rest on the Ground digests or memory, not on fresh checks: EBMT 2023, EASL 2025 SOS prophylaxis, the EASL 2023 pregnancy extracts, the AIH postpartum flare rates, Nery 2015 and Zocco 2009. All engine claims were re-run with the deck chains via src/worker-core.js (scripts in ).

### measuring

- The 12 mmHg bleeding threshold is worded as a cut-off in two places: hvpg/grades ask ('12 mmHg or more') and endoscopy.js:56 ('Bleeding needs an HVPG of about 12 mmHg or more'). That contradicts hvpg/grades notes ('12 is not a cut-off'), varices.js:45, guide 8.6 and the humanizer's rising-risk rule. Reword both to 'the risk rises steeply from about 12 mmHg'.
- MASLD wedge caveat: sites.js:57 says 'In MASLD cirrhosis the wedge can read low, so an HVPG under 10 does not rule out CSPH', but the hvpg deck, the deck that teaches the HVPG, never says it. one-year (an obese MASLD patient) calls an HVPG of 8.8 mmHg 'not CSPH' without it (one-year/cath ask).
- Spleen stiffness wording differs: stiffness.js:51 and one-year.js:39 say 'above 55 kPa', main.js:1857 says '55 kPa or more' (the Echosens summary of Baveno VIII says 55 or more; GI and Hepatology News says over 55). None says '100 Hz'. Baveno VIII's new spleen stiffness under 40 kPa, which spares the scope, appears in no deck (stiffness, spleen, endoscopy.js:31, varices.js:30). Settle the wording against the PDF and add it everywhere at once.
- The scope-sparing rule (under 20 kPa with platelets of 150 or more) is restated in stiffness/rule, spleen/screen, endoscopy.js:31 and varices.js:30. Keep the full statement in endoscopy (the owner of the scope decision) and stiffness. Have spleen teach the spleen-specific version (spleen stiffness under 40 kPa), so the four decks add to each other instead of repeating.
- Model artifact on drug slides: varix size follows the gradient with no lag (src/engine/engine.js:775). Every drug slide with a varix tile shows varices halving at once: one-year/carv (5.0 to 2.5 mm), treatment/prop and treatment/carv (tiles ['varix']), and ncph/nsbb (tiles ['pv', 'varix'], key 'varix'). The model's liver stiffness also falls at once on a beta-blocker (csph: 24.1 to 18.1 kPa on carvedilol, computed). Swap varix tiles on drug slides for map/hr or the wall tool, never show lsm on a drug slide, or add a caveat.
- 'HVPG by level of resistance' is taught three times with near-identical summary tables: hvpg summary (pv, whvp, fhvp, ivc, ra, hvpg, ppg), where.js:121 (the same seven columns) and sites.js:98 (pv, whvp, fhvp, ra, hvpg, ppg). Make the hvpg summary answer 'which number to read instead' (hvpg-12).
- Platelets and spleen are taught twice in almost the same words: stiffness/spleen notes and spleen/platelets (a third of platelets pooled normally, up to 90% in splenomegaly, less thrombopoietin). Stiffness/spleen could point to the spleen deck and add spleen stiffness instead.
- Schistosomiasis appears as a pitfall in hvpg/presin, stiffness/schisto, ncph, beta-blockers/schisto, sites and where-resistance. The vignettes vary well (Egypt, Brazil, Yemen, the Philippines), but the asks in hvpg/presin and stiffness/schisto both leave out a portal vein clot, which the summaries of both decks include.
- Congestion stiffness: stiffness/heart says readings 'above 25 kPa are common' in decompensated heart failure. The literature median is about 12 kPa (Soloveva, J Card Fail 2019). Check right-heart (constrict shows an lsm tile) for the same claim before fixing one deck alone.
- Terms: the stiffness, spleen and one-year decks say 'compensated cirrhosis' where Baveno VIII prefers cACLD for diagnoses made by non-invasive tests. Define cACLD once (stiffness/fibrosis) and use it the same way in all three.
- The csph preset gives the same numbers wherever it appears (LSM 24.1 kPa, platelets 88, HVPG 11.6, varix 3.9 mm): hvpg/route, stiffness/spleen and rule, spleen/splenectomy. The cases quoting 88,000/µL and 24 kPa are consistent. Keep the cases' people different (they are: a 57-year-old man with alcohol-associated cirrhosis, a 63-year-old woman with MASH).
- Shared with the digests and outside these four decks, but touching the same rules: ncph.js:62 still attributes 'no proven advantage for carvedilol' to 'EASL 2025 and Baveno VIII', which the literature digest could not confirm. outflow.js 'soswedge' says 'above 10 mmHg' where EBMT 2023 says 10 or more (as in hvpg summary, hvpg-11).

Found by the verifier:

- stiffness deck, slide 'meal' (stiffness.js:55): an existing bug, which the reviewer 'kept'. It is the only lapse slide in all 27 decks that has a preset and no params. The lapse then plays 'from the slide before's state' (decks.js lapse field; presenter.js:1818 si = stateOf[to-1]), and the chain records a start state only when the slide has params (worker-core.js:344). So the live time-lapse runs the meal ramp on the csph patient of 'spleen' (via the visual 'rule'): the FibroScan and tiles climb from 24.1 to about 25.8 kPa (computed), then ease down to the computed cirrhosis end of 14.8. The stiffness appears to fall after the meal. Fix: add params: { splanchnicTone: 1 } (verified: start LSM 12.5, end 14.8). Check on the preview.
- Engine rule the reviewer missed (affects stiffness-2, stiffness-9 and any future preset+lapse slide): a preset slide with a lapse must also carry params, or it animates the previous patient. The authoring guide's lapse row (guide line 106) should say so.
- stiffness deck, summary notes (stiffness.js:89): 'The spleen and platelets follow portal pressure whatever the level of resistance' contradicts its own next clause and the heart-failure row (PV 20.5 mmHg, platelets 202, spleen 11.8 cm). The engine scales splenic congestion to the portal-to-IVC gradient, not to absolute pressure (engine.js:778-780). Rewrite as 'follow the portal gradient'.
- Tile and table rating: the lsm tile labels 25 kPa or more as 'CSPH' whatever the etiology (table-marks.js:31; guide 8.6 'Key facts' also gives '≥ 25 CSPH' and spleen stiffness '≥ 55 kPa' with no 100 Hz). In one-year's obese-MASLD story, any lsm tile or column at d365 (25.75 kPa) will read 'CSPH', undercutting one-year-3. Change the guide's key facts, and consider 'Ruled in' or a softer label.
- Test fallout of the proposed rewrites. tests/deck-pills.json needs a deliberate UPDATE=1 for stiffness/rule ({hvpg@spleen} dropped), stiffness/fibrosis ({hvpg}), one-year/belly ({spleen}) and stiffness/measure and heart ('(a value)' replaced by tokens). The hvpg/wedge rewrite fails tests/deck-terms.test.js outright (term 'sinusoids'), and the hvpg/ppg rewrite loses the 'portal vein' and 'sinusoids' pills.
- hvpg/presin is already a near-duplicate of sites/presin (same preset, cam 'lobule:triad', lens 'drop', triad callout; sites.js:42-43). The overlap the reviewer flags in hvpg-12 goes beyond the summary tables.
- Verification limits: the WebSearch budget was exhausted for this turn, and WebFetch fails DNS here. So the SRU 2020 rule of four, the Soloveva 2019 heart-failure figures, EBMT 2023 '≥10' against '>10', and whether Baveno VII already had the spleen stiffness 40 kPa rule were checked from memory only, as stated in each verdict.

### imaging

- Near-duplicate cases on the same preset: doppler/reverse (66-year-old woman, MASH, recurrent HE, albumin 2.6 g/dL, cirr-hepatofugal), two-inflows/fugal (two-inflows.js:78: 67-year-old woman, MASH, recurrent HE, albumin 2.6 g/dL, same preset) and shunts/srs (shunts.js:43: 64-year-old woman, MASH, confusion despite lactulose and rifaximin). Also doppler/clot, prehepatic/clot (prehepatic.js:24) and two-inflows:39, all on pvt-acute with 'has had N days of constant ... pain. Her/His liver tests are normal'. Guide section 14 asks to vary the people; at least 10 cases across decks are a woman with MASH cirrhosis.
- TIPS baseline Doppler timing disagrees between decks: doppler/tips says 'a baseline study in the first days', tips-doppler/stent says 'in the first weeks' (tips-doppler.js:58), and shunts/tips gives no timing. Align on 'the first weeks'. Air in a new covered stent can hide it for days, and EASL 2025 follow-up visits start at 4-6 weeks.
- The TIPS target gradient (PPG under 12 mmHg) appears in shunts/tips (shunts.js:49) but not in tips-doppler, which shows the PPG falling to 10.6 mmHg without naming the target.
- Repeated content to cross-reference rather than reteach: - doppler/hepatic and right-heart/normal (normal triphasic hepatic vein, same healthy RHV Doppler with waves); - doppler/rhf and right-heart/pulse (pulsatile portal vein); - doppler/bc and outflow/nowedge (the same Doppler signs of Budd-Chiari); - doppler/smv and prehepatic/cavernoma; - doppler/tips and the tips-doppler deck and shunts/tips; - endoscopy/oct, transfuse and band and variceal-bleed/oct, blood and band (the same octreotide dose, erythromycin, ceftriaxone and Villanueva numbers). The doppler deck works best as the side-by-side 'read the trace' deck, with interpretation asks.
- Hepatic vein waveform wording differs: doppler/hepatic says 'A tall a wave and S-wave reversal point to tricuspid regurgitation or a high right atrial pressure'. right-heart/tr says severe TR shrinks and then reverses the S wave, and a high RA pressure without TR gives a tall a wave. Align doppler to right-heart (Scheinfeld, RadioGraphics 2009).
- Beta-blockers with ascites and varices: endoscopy/large ('still an open question') and recompensation.js:49 both conflict with the new Baveno VIII statement (carvedilol preferred, to prevent a first bleed and ascites complications; LoE 3, strong, 81%; full text not read).
- No deck mentions the Baveno VIII spleen-stiffness route to skip screening endoscopy (SSM under 40 kPa): endoscopy/screen, spleen.js:45 and stiffness.js:51 teach only LSM under 20 kPa with platelets of 150,000/µL or more. The model has no spleen stiffness, so this belongs in notes or lines.
- Pre-emptive TIPS criteria: endoscopy/band leaves out an HVPG of 20 mmHg or more and 'ideally within 24 hours'. variceal-bleed/tips (variceal-bleed.js:71-73) has them.
- Bleeding threshold wording: endoscopy/tension says 'Bleeding needs an HVPG of about 12 mmHg or more'. The guide's key facts and other decks say the risk rises from about 12 mmHg and varices can bleed below it.
- Drug details to verify, not changed by me: - Octreotide infusion is '50 µg an hour' in endoscopy/oct and variceal-bleed/oct, but both literature digests report the AASLD 2024 table as 25-50 µg an hour. I could not confirm this (web search limit reached); if confirmed, write 'then 25 to 50 µg an hour' in both decks. - variceal-bleed/oct gives ceftriaxone 'for up to 5 days', while the digests cite AASLD 2024 and ESGE at up to 7 days and Baveno VIII as individualized.
- Doppler instrument against the notes: the card calls portal pulsatility 0.15-0.5 'Phasic' and over 0.5 'Pulsatile' (doppler.js:295, 303). doppler/rhf teaches VExUS (0.3 abnormal, 0.5 severe) in percent. right-heart/pulse also discusses pulsatility, so the card and both decks should use the same cut-offs and units.
- Guide drift that affects these decks: - deck-authoring-guide.md section 5.2 describes the velocity lens as 'pale amber (slow) to deep brown (fast)', but tokens.css:103-104 runs from amber and pale sand through teal to violet. tips-doppler/before's notes describe the real colors. - Guide section 7.2 omits the DIPS, C1b and C3 probes. - Neither the guide nor the decks.js field reference lists tool.deltaLabel (presenter.js:2028). - The guide's section 2 id list lacks tips-doppler.
- If the balloon tamponade demonstration is added (endoscopy 'rescue' or a new refractory-bleed deck), put the live balloon in one deck only, the one with the scope that draws it (instruments.js:173). variceal-bleed/band should then refer to it rather than duplicate it. Likewise, if varices/umbilical gains a C3 Doppler, teach 'collaterals on imaging diagnose CSPH' in one deck (proposed: doppler/umbilical) and cross-reference it from the other.
- Note for whoever checks numbers: I computed every slide state of these three decks with the engine, using a scratch script that writes nothing to the repo. The script and its output command are at (run: node states.mjs src/ui/decks/<file>.js <EXPORT>). Balloon and rupture checks are in bleed3.mjs in the same folder.

Found by the verifier:

- Doppler card pulsatility includes the breath, which conflicts with the deck's VExUS teaching. Deck: doppler, slides probe and rhf; also right-heart/pulse. Source: doppler.js:244-255, 280, 440; engine.js:290-292 (4 s breath). The card's 'Pulsatility' is swing over peak across a 3 s window, so the healthy portal vein reads about 0.4-0.5 (my run 0.48; 0.03 with respiration off). doppler/rhf notes call 30% or more abnormal. Fix: compute the card value per cardiac cycle, as engine.js:681-692 (pvPI) already does.
- Four near-identical Budd–Chiari cases on the same preset: doppler.js:87, ascites.js:88, outflow.js:23 and sites.js:73. All are young women, and all read 'has had RUQ pain for 2 months and now has tense ascites'. Guide section 14 ('vary the people') applies; the reviewer caught only the reverse and clot duplicates.
- The endoscopy/tension wall card reads 'past the tear point' (wall ratio 1.6 on cirr-decomp; worker-core.js fingerprint wall, instruments.js wall update) for a varix that is not bleeding, because spontaneous rupture is off (scenario.js bleeding false; noRupture in the chain). Add a caveat ('In the model varices never tear on their own; the stress shows how close this one is') or check how it reads on the preview.
- Cross-deck contradiction on the csph preset: pvt-cirrhosis/base is titled 'Slow flow toward a stiff liver' (pvt-cirrhosis.js:23-25), but the model's main portal vein there reads 24.0 cm/s, normal by the doppler deck's 15 cm/s rule. That clashes with the proposed doppler/umbilical ('a normal {pvVel}') and with the Doppler card, which will say 'Normal'.
- Logic flaw repeated in two decks: 'covered stents narrow far less often, so the change from the baseline study counts for more' (doppler.js:107, tips-doppler.js:68). Rarer stenosis does not make the change more informative. The reason is that the bare-stent cut-offs fit covered stents poorly. Reword in both.
- doppler/slow line 'the whole portal vein turns pale' is not what the velocity lens shows. On cirr-decomp the left portal vein reads 23.4 cm/s, inside its 12-35 range (velNormal.js DOPPLER_NORMAL), so it keeps the normal color. Say 'the main portal vein'.
- Several proposed replacement vignettes recreate duplicates: doppler-2 against where.js:25 and pvt-cirrhosis.js:25; endoscopy-9 against treatment.js:17; doppler-5 against two-inflows.js:39 wording. Any new case should be checked by grep against existing cases on the same preset before it is adopted.
- Web search was unavailable to me (the shared search budget was spent and fetch failed DNS), so the Baveno VIII wordings here remain unverified against the full text: NSBB with ascites, spleen stiffness under 40 kPa, stent preferred over balloon, collaterals diagnosing CSPH. I relied on the digests and on Baveno VII from memory.

### varices-bleeding-drugs

- Carvedilol's extra HVPG fall is stated three ways: beta-blockers/carv-sin says 'about 2 mmHg more than propranolol', yet its summary notes cite a 2025 seven-trial meta-analysis that found 0.76 mmHg; treatment/vs notes and one-year/carv say 'about a fifth'; circuit/drugs says 'more'. Use one phrasing everywhere: 'about a fifth, about 1 to 2 mmHg more than propranolol'.
- The attribution 'Baveno VIII and EASL 2025 see no proven advantage for carvedilol' in PSVD appears in beta-blockers (carv-pre caveat, choose notes) and ncph.js:62. Neither digest could confirm the guideline wording. Use the same safe wording in both decks until the PDFs are checked.
- Endoscopy timing omits 'sooner if unstable' in three places: variceal-bleed/band ('once the blood pressure holds'), endoscopy.js:64 and endoscopy/oct line (endoscopy.js:71, 'once the circulation is restored'). Baveno VIII: within 12 hours, as soon as possible if the patient stays unstable.
- The octreotide dose (a 50 µg bolus, then 50 µg an hour) appears in variceal-bleed (line, notes, checks), treatment/oct and endoscopy/oct. If the AASLD 2024 table gives 25 to 50 µg an hour, change all three at once.
- Only variceal-bleed gives an antibiotic duration ('up to 5 days'), which disagrees with AASLD, EASL and Baveno's 'up to 7 days'.
- Pre-emptive TIPS criteria: variceal-bleed/tips includes an HVPG of 20 mmHg or more and the 1 to 2 week late window; endoscopy.js:88 lists only the Child–Pugh criteria.
- Bridge for uncontrolled bleeding: variceal-bleed says the esophageal stent is preferred. That is wrong for US practice, where the balloon tube is the usual bridge. No deck shows the balloon, although the engine models it (balloonEso, balloonGas).
- Overlap 1: varices/fundal and shunts/grs share preset, camera and gastrorenal mark, and their cases are nearly identical (a large fundal varix, albumin 3.0 g/dL). Give varices/fundal the bleeding and treatment angle, and give it a different person.
- Overlap 2: varices/svt and prehepatic/svt share preset, camera, lens, line and ask. Give varices the change-from-healthy lens, and prehepatic the splenic-clot slider.
- Overlap 3: treatment/prop and carv repeat beta-blockers/prop-sin and carv-sin, and circuit/drugs, almost word for word.
- Overlap 4: endoscopy's bleed, oct, transfuse and band slides follow the same sequence as variceal-bleed's tear, oct, blood and band, with the same octreotide and transfusion text.
- Encephalopathy after TIPS is 'about a third' in treatment (cost line, notes and checks). EASL 2025 and Bureau 2021 support 'a third to a half in the first year'; align shunts and tips-doppler if they quote a rate.
- Banding every 2 to 4 weeks until eradication is consistent in treatment, variceal-bleed and endoscopy. No change is needed.
- Code gaps that show to learners: table COLS and CUT lack 'hb' and 'gv' (src/ui/table-marks.js:102, 104). The variceal-bleed summary therefore heads a column 'hb', and no table can show a fundal varix. The Doppler probe label 'Paraumb. C3' (src/ui/doppler.js:41) exposes an internal edge id.
- Slide-chain behavior that matters to any bleeding deck: a slide with params settles twice (src/worker-core.js:336, 352). An open bleed runs through both settles, and the chain never clots it (src/engine/engine.js:570), so slides with params bleed longer, and a balloon or rescue TIPS needs an explicit stopBleed for the computed chain to match the live clock.
- Rule 1 (one sentence per line) is broken in 35 of the 38 lines in these four decks. Most often the second sentence is the house pattern 'Colored by X, the ... thin.' Either fold that sentence in after a semicolon, as in the rewrites above, or change the guide to allow 'the point, then what the figure shows' as one line of up to two sentences. The notes also often run 5 to 7 sentences (variceal-bleed/oct and blood are about 145 words).
- Web search limit: the shared search budget ran out during this review. The octreotide 25 to 50 µg an hour figure, the 4 mmHg MAP difference, US availability of the esophageal stent and the PSVD guideline wording could not be re-checked, and are marked low or medium confidence above.

Found by the verifier:

- beta-blockers 'choose' notes (beta-blockers.js:81) say 'With ascites, or whenever the systolic pressure stays below 90 mmHg or acute kidney injury develops, the dose is reduced or the drug stopped.' Ascites alone is not a reason to reduce the dose, and the sentence contradicts the one before it ('It also prefers carvedilol with ascites and varices'). Replace with: 'With ascites, reduce or stop it if the systolic pressure stays under 90 mmHg, the MAP under 65 mmHg, or HRS-AKI develops.' Source: Baveno VII and VIII per digests A and B.
- Table colors, src/ui/table-marks.js:110 and 140: UP_GOOD lacks 'hb', so any 'vs' table colors a falling hemoglobin green ('better'); effOf('hb', ↓) returns 'good'. Today the variceal-bleed summary hides it only because Hb stays at 13.5-13.7 g/dL. Fix it together with the COLS/CUT entries in variceal-bleed-5.
- beta-blockers summary: the 'res' column the review proposes would show '•' for carvedilol in both diseases, because the cirrhosis fall (9.5 to 8.1) is under STEP_REL.res = 1.5 (table-marks.js:126) and non-reference rows show only arrows (presenter.js:2246-2252). It needs STEP_REL.res = 1.2.
- varices/umbilical: lens 'direction' colors the paraumbilical vein 'Usual', because C3 is defined LPV→EPI (topology.js:152; stage.js:4228-4232). It cannot show 'away from the liver'. The C3 Doppler reads 'Portal → systemic' (doppler.js:339-342).
- varices/eso: the proposed rewrite 'left gastric (coronary)' breaks the lgv term pill, whose words are 'left gastric vein|coronary vein' (presenter.js:134).
- treatment/tips: the proposed line is 48 words and fails tests/deck-line-length.test.js (any line of 45 words or more fails).
- treatment: moving 'vs' to after 'tips' (treatment-1) leaves the 'Compared' kicker between the 'Shunt' and 'After TIPS' sections (treatment.js:5), so the running head jumps. The 'after' ask answer (treatment.js:73) repeats the kidney claim the review removes from the line.
- Model behavior for any BRTO slide: in the gastric-varix patient, closing C5 also closes C2 (topology.js:200), yet the fundal varix tile grows from 8.9 to 10.9 mm and the esophageal varix to 8.7 mm on settle. Never show a gv tile after BRTO. shunts/brto already avoids it with tiles ['pv','varix'].
- Proposed refractory-bleed deck: (a) the first slide's rupture is copied onto the Outline (decks.js:122-135), so the opening figure is already bleeding; (b) the summary's vs:'first' compares the gastric-varix patient with the cirr-decomp patient; (c) the 'gv' vignette (a woman with hepatitis C and melena from a fundal varix) re-creates the shunts/grs and varices/fundal overlap; (d) the gastric balloon goes up first in practice, which the model cannot show; (e) do not name Baveno VII in learner text for the futility rule or the 24-hour balloon limit.
- varices summary (varices-1): the proposed line 'Esophageal varices grow with the PPG' is contradicted by the table's own fundal row (PPG 17.5 mmHg, esophageal varix 2.0 mm 'None').
- Cross-deck item 1 overstates the inconsistency: 'about a fifth' (treatment.js:95, one-year.js:74) is carvedilol's total HVPG fall, while 'about 2 mmHg more than propranolol' (beta-blockers.js:48) is the extra fall over propranolol. Only the beta-blockers figure conflicts with the 2025 meta-analysis.
- variceal-bleed/before notes (variceal-bleed.js:30) say the beat trace runs 'for the rest of the talk'. That becomes untrue if variceal-bleed-10's scope replaces it on 'band'.
- Engine fact shown but not called out as a defect: settle is documented as 'not elapsed physiological time' (engine.js:694-695), yet every settle drains 343 s of bleeding (engine.js:452-456, 696-701). Any future slide with params in a bleeding chain silently loses extra blood. Worth a comment in decks.js or a test, rather than an engine change that would re-tune variceal-bleed and endoscopy.

Scratch scripts are in (chain.mjs, p-*.mjs, rb.mjs, wc.mjs).

### ascites-decompensation

- Beta-blockers once ascites is present: recompensation.js:49 and endoscopy.js:47 say any benefit beyond preventing bleeding is 'still open'. beta-blockers.js:81 says Baveno VIII 'also prefers carvedilol with ascites and varices'. Align all three to the Baveno VIII new statement: carvedilol preferred with ascites and varices, to prevent a first bleed and ascites-related complications, with the stop rules SBP under 90, MAP under 65 or HRS-AKI.
- TIPS is taught in four decks: treatment (tips, after, cost, vs), tap/tips, shunts (tips, wide, occl) and tips-doppler. The contraindication ask appears in both treatment/cost and tap/tips. The HE rate is 'about a third' in treatment.js:79 and 'one in three' in tap.js:69, while the literature says a third to a half in the first year. Suggested split: treatment covers the mechanism against drugs; tap covers selection for recurrent or refractory ascites (Bureau 2017, the EASL 2025 work-up, rifaximin); shunts covers stent width, encephalopathy and shunt closure. Use one HE figure everywhere.
- ascites/hf and tap/heart are the same slide: same rhf preset, same tiles, near-identical asks (ascites.js:83, tap.js:79). ascites checks[1] and tap checks[2] are also near-identical. The SAAG-protein pattern is taught again in sites, where-resistance, right-heart and lobule. Keep the ascites deck as its home and cut tap/heart.
- Same ramp, different clock: ascites/fill runs cirrhosis 0.6 to 0.85 and albumin 4 to 2.8 over 60 days; recompensation/ascites runs the identical ramp over 365 days. Use a year in both.
- Diuretic max doses are asked in both tap/diuretics and recompensation/diuretics. The tap notes also muddle AASLD's combined start with EASL's spironolactone-first start.
- Engine caution for the ground-phase ideas: infused volume never leaves the model, because it has no kidneys (engine.js:900-921). In the tap chain, one 'infuse albumin' after the tap makes the ascites return to 6.0 L in 14 days, with RA 9.1 mmHg, against 3.7 L now and 5.2 L with no albumin. That reverses the teaching. Do not use infuse (albumin or plasma) on a slide that is followed by days or further params, unless the numbers are checked.
- Closing a shunt (occluded C5 in shunts/brto, or C6 if embolization is added) enlarges the esophageal varices at once, because varixMature reads the coronary vein's existing remodeling (engine.js:1037-1042). Any deck reusing the BRTO compare, as proposed for varices/fundal, needs the caveat that in patients this takes months.
- Fundal varices and BRTO appear in varices/fundal (varices.js:52-54) and in shunts grs and brto. The asks complement each other (which vein, what to watch for), but keep the management wording the same: glue (off-label in the US), BRTO, PARTO or CARTO, or TIPS. Neither BRTO nor TIPS is used to prevent a first fundal bleed.
- Similar case cards: a young woman with 2 months of RUQ pain and tense ascites in ascites/bc and sites.js:73; a MASH woman with recurrent HE despite lactulose and rifaximin in shunts/srs and doppler.js:60. Vary age, sex and history, per guide section 14.
- SBP threshold wording: '250 or more' is correct. ascites/saag says 'more than 250' and tap/first says 'over 250'.
- The model's HE index is only shunt fraction x (0.6 + 0.8 x cirrhosis) (metrics.js:69). A new 'he' tile would add little beyond 'shunt'. Typed encephalopathy-risk words in tables should follow the 'liver' column, so they do not contradict the 'Shunted' numbers.
- Review limit: the web-search budget ran out at the start of this review. Clinical items rely on the two shared literature digests, the code and memory, and are marked medium or low where unverified: the Farias BNP cut-off, DIVERT HE outcome, passive stent expansion timing, the EASL 2025 gradient target for ascites, and Baveno VIII Child-Pugh A for recompensation.

Found by the verifier:

- shunts/brto notes (shunts.js:84) say 'In the model the fundal varix keeps its size because the sclerosant is not modeled'. It does not: the gv tile goes from 8.9 mm on grs to 10.9 mm on brto (verified run). Fix: 'In the model the fundal varix even swells, because the sclerosant that fills and clots it is not modeled; only the pressure effect is real.'
- shunts/tips (shunts.js:49-50): the 'shunt' tile reads 93% at every stent width (6 mm 93.3%, 8 mm 93.3%, 10 mm 93.3%, 12 mm 93.4%; verified), so under the slide's own whatIf it stays flat while the deck says wider stents divert more. Only PPG (12.5 to 7.6 mmHg) and liver flow (41 to 23%) move. Replace 'shunt' with 'varix' in tiles, or drop it.
- tap/lvp and tap/albumin: the engine's paracentesis action takes albumin: true (engine.js:953-958). It skips the PICD volume loss and raises serum albumin by 0.3 g/dL per 5 L. Neither the deck nor the review uses it. Verified: with albumin, HR after the tap is 91.6 against 94.3 without, MAP 93.8 against 93.4, and the fluid back at 14 days is 3.95 L. So the model's HR rise after the tap is mostly decompression, not PICD. Do not present the HR tile as the PICD marker.
- ascites/presin line (ascites.js:74) says the portal-tract resistance 'leaves sinusoidal pressure near normal', but the model's sin is 9.5 mmHg, rated amber 'High' (table-marks.js:12, amber at 9 or more) on the slide's own sin tile. Say 'only mildly raised, at {sin}'.
- ascites/bc (ascites.js:86): any patient-compare added there (review ascites-7) keeps the slide's 'Clot in hepatic veins' mark on the other patient. livePatient (presenter.js:1487-1503) does not change marks. The same risk applies to the shunts/dsrs compare (S_DSR 'Warren shunt' mark under the Portocaval and Mesocaval buttons).
- recompensation/summary line (recompensation.js:79) says 'Carvedilol continues while CSPH persists', while the table's HVPG for 'Cause removed' and 'Off diuretics' is 9.2-9.3 mmHg, rated 'Mild', on carvedilol. On screen this says CSPH has resolved. Review item recompensation-2 addresses it; the 'keep' slide or a footnote is needed, not optional.
- Review method: the web-search budget for this turn was exhausted (limit reached), and WebFetch failed DNS (pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov). Clinical verdicts rest on the two shared digests and memory: Farias BNP 364 pg/mL, the DIVERT encephalopathy result, Simon-Talero 60%, the Baveno VIII carvedilol-with-ascites statement, the EASL 2025 gradient target for ascites and the alfapump approval remain unverified against primary text. Model numbers were all re-run with tests/deck-states.js runSteps; scripts are in

### Render check, across decks

- Test conditions: 4 CPUs at load average 10-16, mostly from other agents' Chromium sessions. Pages ran at 1-3 fps, a full screenshot took 2-48 s, and phone and iPad runs were 3 to 10 times slower than desktop. The DOM pop detector cannot judge fades shorter than about 500 ms at this frame rate. 'Eases' therefore rests on intermediate values captured during each change (tiles, FibroScan canvas, Doppler text), not on frame-by-frame smoothness. Every 'eases' verdict should be checked on the owner's devices.
- A lapse whose slide has a preset but no params plays from the previous slide's patient (presenter.js:1818; worker-core.js:344). A scan of all 17 lapse slides found only stiffness/meal affected. Any new lapse with its own patient must carry params, and its ramp's first values must match the chain's state. Otherwise day 1 jumps, as in the lobule prototype (protein 1.7 to 2.4 'High') and the pregnancy prototype (gestation 0 back to 37).
- On a lapse slide the line's {tokens} show the end values from the first frame, while the tiles count up from the start (presenter.js:1866). Seen on the one-year d90 prototype ('21.3 kPa' beside a tile reading 17.8). Word lapse lines as the end point ('Three months on, ...').
- Patient buttons ({ label, preset }) land on computed states and eased both ways on stiffness/schisto and ascites/bc. Param buttons (liveSet) stop updating the tiles after 7 s (presenter.js:1529) and freeze mid-ease for slow variables (two-inflows/switch: 31-39% against a settled 38.5%). For recommended compares, prefer { preset, params } buttons.
- Slide-level marks and glows stay when a patient button swaps the patient (presenter.js:1487-1503, 1921). ascites/bc showed 'Clot in hepatic veins' on a heart-failure patient. Avoid patient buttons on marked slides until compare options can carry their own mark.
- Threshold words flicker at their cut-offs: the Doppler pattern word (Phasic/Monophasic at pi 0.15) in cirrhosis on doppler/slow and pvt-cirrhosis/base, and the scope grade (None/F1, once F2 Large with Red wale) during one-year/d180. Hysteresis in the shared instrument code would fix every deck at once.
- On phone, a slide with a tool shows only the instrument and hides the tiles: the liver-flow tile on two-inflows/switch, the platelet and spleen tiles on spleen/pse, and the stiffness tiles on stiffness/meal. Recommendations whose point is a tile change are invisible on the iPhone unless the slide drops its tool.
- Learner-facing naming: the C3 Doppler is titled 'PARAUMBILICAL VEIN' (desktop), 'UMBILICAL V.' (phone) and 'Paraumb. C3' (footer, an internal code). The flow-time chart is always titled 'Portal pressure and flow over time' (presenter-tools.js:45), even when it plots sinusoid pressure and ascites. ascites/bc on desktop labels the right atrium twice (organ label plus station).
- The corner credit relocated (top, mid-figure or top-right) and appeared at once when compare rows were added or patients swapped. That is by design in credit.js:1-3 (never animated), but it is a visible change whenever a button row is added to a slide.
